About The Stat Line

The Stat Line is an independent sports and culture desk. One stop for sports, gaming, entertainment, collectibles and fashion — built as an autoblog: stories are spotted as they happen, verified, and published automatically. No waiting on a news cycle.

Every story on this site is verified real before it goes up. Stats get re-checked against the box score, historical claims get the full year-by-year treatment, and news gets corroborated — false alarms get killed quietly. You’ll never see them.

Entertainment · Nov 12, 2026 · 3:36 PM ET

Fat Perez, Riggs and Bobby Ray win the Internet Invitational 5 & 3

By Matt

Blue never trailed at Payne's Valley — Fat Perez, Riggs and Bobby Ray closed out Big John, Ryan Whitney and Salim 5 & 3 to split the $4 million purse and lift the Cody 'Beef' Franke Trophy.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Blue never trailed

The Internet Invitational is over, and it belongs to Team Blue. Fat Perez, Sam "Riggs" Bozoian and Bobby Ray beat Big John, Ryan Whitney and Salim Sirur 5 & 3 in the 18-hole alternate-shot final at Payne's Valley, closing the match out on the 15th hole with three still to play.

The margin tells the whole story. Blue went 1 up on the first hole and never gave it back — 2 up after three, 3 up at the turn, 4 up after ten, and 5 up after fifteen. Red never led a single hole all day. In a format built to swing wildly, with three wildly different handicaps sharing every shot, this one never really swung at all.

Each of the three winners takes home $1,338,333 from the $4 million purse, and Bobby Ray's total runs $25,000 higher — the 9th-hole money he banked alongside The Duke back on Day 2. On top of the split, the trio also takes home a Tracker boat from Bass Pro Shops. The trophy they lifted is the Cody "Beef" Franke Trophy, named for last year's winner.

The drama: Salim, and the finale nobody could look away from

The score says Blue won 5 & 3. The internet says something else decided it — or at least ruined the watch. Salim Sirur, Red's third man, drank through the episode on camera: a tequila shot on just about every hole, beers on top of it, asking the cart girl for another round. Australian Golf Digest's episode recap named him "the drunkest man alive" and described him as "blackout drunk" going into the final, showing no signs of stopping.

It bled into the team. At one point Salim took a beer out of Big John's hand, causing real friction inside the group, and Ryan Whitney spent part of the match coaching Big John on keeping Salim's vibes up rather than letting it show. Big John, for his part, came out of it as the sympathetic figure of the finale — viewers calling him "all heart" and the MVP of a losing team.

The audience reaction has been brutal. Across Barstool's, Portnoy's and the Invitational's own posts, the comments are dominated by viewers calling the finale unwatchable, blaming Salim's drinking for costing his teammates $1.3 million each, and calling for him not to be invited back. Some of it is cruel; a smaller thread defends him or jokes about it. The dominant tone is anger that a $4 million match came down to a guy who could barely stand up straight.

There is a twist that complicates the whole thing: Salim himself has said he's on Day 87 of a 90-day sober challenge — he knew before the episode aired that he needed a lifestyle change. Golf Digest called the whole thing "an all-time drunk golf performance" while noting he's since reeled the drinking in. Whether the finale becomes a turning point for him or just the episode everyone remembers him for is the open question hanging over next year.

The trophy means something

That name is the emotional center of this whole series. Cody "Beef" Franke won the first Internet Invitational in 2025 alongside Brad Dalke and Francis Ellis, beating Paige Spiranac, Frankie Borrelli and Malosi Togisala 1 up in the final. He died suddenly at 31 in October 2025, the day before the series aired. Barstool and the Fore Play crew helped set up a scholarship fund in his name at his alma mater, Ferris State, and his family opened this year's first episode to introduce the Beef Franke Foundation, which funds golf-management scholarships and a Heart of the Course Award for the people who keep courses running. They finished by teaching the room Beef's signature send-off: two claps and a woo.

So when the trophy got handed over at Payne's Valley, it wasn't just a prop with a funny name. It was the whole point of the week.

How Blue got here

Nobody had this trio winning it when the field of 64 was drafted. All six finalists came out of Ryan Ruffels' Team White, which survived a wild three days at Big Cedar Lodge in Ridgedale, Missouri.

Day 1 at Ozarks National ended with Ruffels and Peter Finch's team tied 8–8, and Ruffels going through on a sudden-death playoff — Big John, nearly left out of the event entirely, holing the putt on the second extra hole. Defending champions Dalke's Red lost 10.5–5.5 to Wesley Bryan's Green and went home. Day 2 at Buffalo Ridge was sixteen singles matches, and Ruffels closed it out 12.5–6.5, with Salim winning the 17th and 18th to clinch it on the last.

Then came the twist: four of Ruffels' sixteen were going home that night. The captains played a sudden-death hole under the lights, and Grant Horvat's team went out when Ryan Whitney's par beat Jake Adams on the next hole — taking Horvat, Adams, Spiranac and Jon Gruden with it. The last twelve were split into four teams of three for nine holes of alternate-shot stroke play at Payne's Valley, top two through. Blue won it at two over. Red got through at four over only because Salim holed a bogey putt on the last that would have tied Green if he'd missed.

On paper, the final was close: Blue's combined handicap averaged 2.1 (Fat Perez 0.3, Riggs 4.1, Bobby Ray 2.0) against Red's 2.9 (Big John +3.1, Whitney 2.1, Salim 9.8). A million simulations gave Blue a 57% edge. On the course, it wasn't close at all.

What this event is

For anyone still catching up: the Internet Invitational is Barstool Sports and Bob Does Sports' creator golf tournament, hosted by Dave Portnoy, who runs the show as host and rules commissioner alongside Big Cat and former PGA Tour pro Kevin Kisner. The second edition filmed August 10–17, 2026 at Big Cedar Lodge and rolled out in six episodes on YouTube — Mondays and Thursdays, October 26 through today — free on the Fore Play Golf, Bob Does Sports and Internet Invitational channels, presented by Dunkin'.

The first two episodes alone drew more than 16 million combined views. It's the rare YouTube golf series that plays like a real sports event: a draft, team play, a rules committee with actual teeth — the "Gruden Rule" lets them veto any pairing that looks like a thrown match — and side pots everywhere, including a $500,000 Reese's hole-in-one prize on the par 3 each day and $25,000-a-man payouts on the Day 2 9th hole. The production team cut it from more than 24 terabytes of raw footage, and the 2025 series was a Shorty Awards finalist for Best Video Series.

It also never stops being messy, which is half the appeal. The Duke, the weakest golfer in the 64-man field, became the story of Episode 5 when his play cost Ruffels and Marissa Wenzler their shot at the final — then took to Instagram to apologize to his teammates. Portnoy spent Wednesday addressing whether The Duke gets invited back at all.

What's next

Portnoy has confirmed the Invitational will be an annual event, and the 2026 edition proved the format has legs: a bigger field, a purse four times the size of year one, and a final that delivered a clean, decisive winner. The champions list now reads two lines long — Dalke, Ellis and Beef in 2025; Fat Perez, Riggs and Bobby Ray in 2026. Three guys who started the week as long shots walk away as millionaires, and the trophy they lifted carries the name of the guy who won it first.

Entertainment · Nov 12, 2026 · 1:09 PM ET

Streamer Awards go live tonight in Los Angeles

By Matt

Streaming's biggest night is tonight — the sixth annual Streamer Awards, live free on Twitch and YouTube, with IShowSpeed, Jynxzi, and Kai Cenat leading the nominations and one very stolen set of trophies hanging over the show.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Streaming's biggest night

The Streamer Awards go live tonight from Los Angeles, with the main show at 8:30 p.m. ET streaming free on Twitch and YouTube. The red carpet starts at 5:30 p.m. ET, and the whole night is hosted by the show's founder, QTCinderella, alongside Caroline Kwan.

This is the sixth edition of the show, and the largest one yet: 37 categories covering creators across Twitch, YouTube, and Kick. Fan voting counted for 70% of each result, with an industry panel deciding the remaining 30%. Voting ran October 16 through October 31, so tonight is purely about the reveals.

The names to watch

IShowSpeed, Jynxzi, and Kai Cenat lead the field with four nominations each. Speed is the defending Streamer of the Year, and he is on the ballot again — joined by CaseOh, Joe Bartolozzi, Jynxzi, and Stable Ronaldo in the flagship category, presented by YouTube this year.

Kai Cenat's four nominations all sit outside that flagship race: the two-time past winner is not on the Streamer of the Year ballot at all, which was one of the biggest talking points when the nominees were revealed on October 16. The other one was xQc, who got zero nominations across all 37 categories and spent the night voting for Speed instead.

Beyond the top prize, the marquee races include Best Marathon Stream — Speed and Kai's Minecraft Marathon against Cinna's Cinnathon, DDG's Hit-A-Thon, JasonTheWeen's 7 Days Stranded, and the Chained Together run from Jynxzi, Stable Ronaldo, and LosPollosTV — plus Streamer University, Kai's summer event, up for Best Streamed Event.

The trophies were stolen

Yes, really. On October 29, QTCinderella announced on X that this year's trophies had been stolen off one of the show's producers' front porches after delivery, and asked anyone in the Los Angeles area who spots them at a Goodwill or pawn shop to let her know. The good news for the results: winners' nameplates aren't attached until the night before the ceremony, so nothing was spoiled — the thief got a box of very blank trophies. QT joked the winners' plates might end up on coffee mugs instead.

The show itself was also moved up a week, from November 17 to tonight, to get clear of the GTA VI release. And for the first time, Kick streamers were eligible for nominations this year.

How to watch

The red carpet starts at 5:30 p.m. ET and the main ceremony follows at 8:30 p.m. ET, streaming free on QTCinderella's Twitch and YouTube channels. Co-streaming is open to everyone — QT confirmed on November 11 that all co-streams are welcome, on any platform, as long as "Streamer Awards" is in the title.

Watch

Watch on YouTube

MLB · Nov 12, 2026 · 8:08 AM ET

Red Sox inquire about Seiya Suzuki

By Matt

Boston has checked in on free agent Seiya Suzuki, per Pete Abraham — the Cubs slugger is coming off the best season of his career and would add the power bat the Sox lineup lacked.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The first bat checked

The Red Sox have checked in on free agent Seiya Suzuki, according to Pete Abraham of the Boston Globe. Free agency opened on November 2, and Suzuki is the first premier bat Boston has reportedly asked about.

The inquiry came out of the GM Meetings in Las Vegas, where Abraham also reported the worst-kept secret in the room: Boston is shopping left fielder Jarren Duran. The two moves fit together — if Duran goes, there's an outfield spot and a middle-of-the-order hole to fill, and Suzuki is the top right-handed bat on the market.

What Suzuki did in 2026

Suzuki, 32, just finished the best season of his five-year run with the Cubs. He set a career high with 5.1 wins above replacement while playing 143 games, and he did it as the middle-of-the-order bat Chicago leaned on all year.

Games: 143

Slash line: .270/.368/.473

Home runs: 26

Doubles: 31

RBIs: 86

OPS: .841

That production is exactly what Boston's lineup was missing. The Sox finished 12th in the American League in home runs last season, and the front office has made adding a middle-of-the-lineup hitter its offseason priority. This isn't the first time the two sides have been linked, either — The Athletic's Jen McCaffrey named Suzuki as a possible Boston target last week.

What it would cost

Suzuki just completed a five-year, $85 million deal he signed with the Cubs ahead of the 2022 season. Chicago extended him a one-year, $23.1 million qualifying offer, but his agent, Joel Wolfe, said at the GM Meetings that Suzuki will decline it and test the open market.

The early projections put the price in nine figures: Spotrac projects a four-year deal worth just over $109 million, while The Athletic's Jim Bowden projects five years and $120 million. Boston won't be bidding alone — the Mariners, Padres, Phillies and Blue Jays are all reported to have interest, and the Cubs are expected to make an effort to keep him.

There's also a draft-pick cost attached. Because Suzuki received a qualifying offer, signing him would cost Boston a second- and fifth-round pick in the 2027 MLB Draft plus $1 million in international signing bonus pool space.

TSL Bets · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:57 AM ET

TSL Bets: The Thursday parlay (~+550) — Kaprizov to score, Wild moneyline, Avalanche-Senators Over 6.5

By Matt

Three legs, three different bets: Kirill Kaprizov to score in Toronto, the Wild to beat a reeling Leafs team, and goals in Ottawa where the over is 9-1 in the last ten meetings. About +550.

Leg 1: Kirill Kaprizov anytime goal (+105)

Minnesota Wild (13-3-4) at Toronto Maple Leafs (8-10-2) — 7:00 PM ET, Scotiabank Arena, Toronto

Wild -170, total 6.5.

Injuries — Minnesota: Filip Gustavsson is on injured reserve with a hip injury. Brock Faber is out with a skull fracture — he has resumed skating but is not expected back until Saturday. Jared Spurgeon is out with a lower-body injury. Michael McCarron is questionable with an upper-body injury.

Injuries — Toronto: Morgan Rielly could return after missing two games with a lower-body injury. Kirill Marchenko and Colton Sissons remain sidelined with lower-body injuries. Max Domi, Miles Wood, Nick Blankenburg, Elvis Merzlikins, Cade Webber, Marshall Rifai and William Villeneuve are all out.

Storylines: Kaprizov has 17 goals — second in the NHL — and 32 points in 20 games. He has scored in five of the Wild's last six road games, and he scored the overtime winner 21 seconds in at Montreal on Tuesday, extending Minnesota's point streak to nine games. The Leafs have lost four straight, allowed 17 goals four games into a six-game homestand, and are still looking for their first win of November. Backup Anthony Stolarz could get the nod in net, with the starter having lost five straight. Kaprizov against a battered Toronto defense is the best goalscorer bet on the board tonight.

Leg 2: Wild moneyline (-170)

Same game, same reasoning, different bet. Minnesota is 13-3-4, riding a nine-game point streak, with the league's second-leading goalscorer and a defenseman in Quinn Hughes who just became the second-fastest blueliner in league history to 80 points with a franchise — 68 games. Toronto is 8-10-2, has lost four straight, and is missing half its defense corps. The Wild are road favorites for a reason, and the -170 is the price of betting the best form in the league against the worst.

Leg 3: Avalanche at Senators Over 6.5 (+100)

Colorado Avalanche (11-6-2) at Ottawa Senators (11-6-2) — 7:00 PM ET, Canadian Tire Centre, Ottawa

Avalanche -120, total 6.5, Over +100 (Fanatics).

Injuries — Colorado: Josh Manson is on injured reserve (long-term) and Zachary L'Heureux is on injured reserve. Nathan MacKinnon is healthy and leads the team with 30 points.

Injuries — Ottawa: Andre Burakovsky, Warren Foegele and William Eklund are on injured reserve. Nick Cousins is out. Linus Ullmark is day-to-day with a lower-body strain — he is 7-1-0 with a 1.33 goals-against average and a .945 save percentage when he plays, which is exactly why this leg carries a warning label.

Storylines: The over is 13-6 in Colorado games this season, and 9-1 in the last ten meetings between these two teams. The Avalanche average 4.11 goals a game, just won 6-3 in Toronto for their second straight win, and have scored 10 goals in two games. Ottawa lost 3-2 in overtime to Washington on Tuesday and is banged up up front. If Ullmark plays, the over gets harder — he is the best goalie in this matchup by a wide margin. If he sits, two offenses that score for fun get a backup. Either way, seven goals is the number, and these two teams have cleared it in nine of their last ten meetings.

The bet

Parlay: Kaprizov anytime goal (+105) + Wild moneyline (-170) + Avalanche-Senators Over 6.5 (+100) — about +550.

A prop, a favorite, and a total — three different kinds of bets, and none of them copies a single from today. The Kaprizov goal and the Wild moneyline are correlated, which is the point: if Minnesota wins in Toronto, the odds are good that Kaprizov is the reason. The over is the independent leg, and it is built on the strongest totals trend on the board tonight.

The honest caveat

Two of the three legs live in the same game, so a bad night in Toronto kills the ticket twice over. Kaprizov can be held off the scoresheet — it happens to everyone — and the Wild are missing their starting goalie and their best defenseman. The over is the riskiest leg on its own terms: if Ullmark plays, a 1.33 goals-against average is a real obstacle to seven goals. A parlay is a parlay: three things have to go right, and one of them is a goalie decision nobody has confirmed yet. Bet accordingly.

The pick

Kaprizov anytime goal + Wild moneyline + Avalanche-Senators Over 6.5 — about +550. A $10 ticket returns about $65. A scorer, a favorite, and the best over trend on the board.

If you or someone you know has a gambling problem, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

TSL Bets · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:57 AM ET

TSL Bets: Bruins moneyline (-128) vs Canadiens — 8-1 at home against a banged-up rival

By Matt

Boston is 13-4-1, 8-1-0 at home, and allowing 2.3 goals a game. Montreal hasn't lost in regulation in a month — but the Habs are without Demidov and their goalie picture is unsettled. Take the Bruins at home.

The game

Montreal Canadiens (11-3-4) at Boston Bruins (13-4-1) — 7:00 PM ET, TD Garden, Boston, ESPN+

Bruins moneyline -128, Canadiens +106, total 6.5 (FanDuel).

Injuries — Boston: James Hagens is on injured reserve, est. return November 21. That is the whole list — the Bruins are otherwise healthy.

Injuries — Montreal: Ivan Demidov is on injured reserve with an ankle injury, est. return November 14 — he has played two games all season. Mike Matheson is day-to-day with a lower-body injury. Samuel Montembeault is on injured reserve, with a return imminent. Nick Suzuki and Jake Evans both missed Wednesday's practice for treatment, with no diagnosis and no timeline given. Jacob Fowler has taken the number one job in net: 8-1-2, 2.14 goals-against average, .925 save percentage.

Storylines: This is first against second in the Atlantic — Boston with 27 points, Montreal with 26 — and both teams are playing well. The Bruins have won eight of nine home games and are giving up only 2.3 goals a game, with eight forwards at 10-plus points and 5-plus goals. The Canadiens have points in 11 straight games (8-0-3), have not lost in regulation since October 14, and are 6-1-1 on the road. Lane Hutson leads them with 18 points, Cole Caufield has 11 goals, and Nick Suzuki has 16 points. Boston has won seven of the last ten meetings, and five of the last six in Boston. The models split the difference — one has Boston at 65% to win, the market has it closer to a coin flip, and the -128 sits in between.

The bet

Bruins moneyline (-128). Home ice, the healthier roster, the better defense, and a head-to-head edge in this building. Montreal's point streak is real, but it is built on overtime and shootout points as much as wins — and the Habs are doing it without their best young forward, with a day-to-day top defenseman and a goalie situation that is still sorting itself out. Boston at -128 is a fair price for the better team at home.

The honest caveat

Montreal has not lost in regulation in a month, and Fowler is not a backup playing above his head — he is 8-1-2 with a .925 save percentage and has taken the job outright. The Canadiens are 6-1-1 on the road, they score 3.5 goals a game, and this is a rivalry game where the records barely matter. If Suzuki and Evans are fine after their treatment day, the Habs are close to full strength at forward. The -128 is a fair price, not a steal — this is a bet on home ice and health, and both of those edges are real but thin.

The pick

Bruins moneyline (-128). A $10 ticket returns about $17.81. The healthier team, at home, with the better defense.

Nike Air Bakin 'Varsity Royal' drops today — $170, and the last colorway sold out

By Matt

The Nike Air Bakin 'Varsity Royal' drops today, November 12 — $170 via Nike SNKRS and select retailers. The retro run keeps rolling, and the 'Varsity Red' before it sold out and is going above retail.

The drop

The Nike Air Bakin "Varsity Royal" drops today, Thursday, November 12 — $170 in men's sizing, available via Nike SNKRS and select retailers including Foot Locker and Hibbett. The style code is IQ5365-400, and pairs go live at 9 a.m. CT.

The colorway is exactly what the name promises: royal blue and white with black accents, on the chunky high-top silhouette Nike has been bringing back all year. The Air Bakin's renewed retro run has been one of the quieter success stories of 2026 — no hype machine, just a classic basketball shoe returning in clean colorways.

Why this one

The Bakin is a 1997 Eric Avar design, famously worn by Tim Hardaway with the Miami Heat — his unofficial signature shoe, even though Nike never officially called it one. The original run was recalled after the flame-style "Air" branding on the spine drew complaints that it resembled "Allah" in Arabic; every retro since has carried standard Nike Air text instead. The shoe has had limited retros in 2007, 2014 and 2023, and the 2026 return has been building all year — Tim Hardaway Jr. even wore the OG colorway in his Heat debut on October 22.

The warning sign is the last drop. The "Varsity Red" sold out and is currently going for above retail on aftermarket sites, which is why the advice on this one is simple: if you want it, do not wait until the afternoon. The royal colorway has the same ingredients — an OG-style two-tone upper, a recognizable silhouette, a $170 price that still feels fair for a retro high-top.

Today is a busy release day — the Alphafly 4 in Total Orange ($280), the Vans Pearlized Old Skool 36 in Marshmallow and Lime Pop ($130), the adidas Bad Bunny BadBo 1.1 ($120) — but the Bakin is the one with a sellout on its record. That is the one to move on first.

Where to get it

Nike SNKRS is the first stop, with Foot Locker, Hibbett and select retailers carrying it as well. Men's sizing only.

Collectibles · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:55 AM ET

Pokémon's Day & Night boxes are trading at double retail a week after launch — and the market is already cooling

By Matt

The Pokémon TCG 30th Celebration Ultra-Premium Collections — Day with Espeon ex, Night with Umbreon ex — launched November 6 at $179.99. Pre-release market prices ran past three times retail, and even after a pullback they are still trading well above it.

The boxes

The peak product of Pokémon's 30th anniversary year landed on November 6: the 30th Celebration Ultra-Premium Collections, in two versions — Day, built around Espeon ex, and Night, built around Umbreon ex. Each one retails for $179.99 at the Pokémon Center and participating retailers.

What is inside justifies the price tag, at least on paper. Every box carries 30 packs — 29 from the 30th Celebration set plus one Classic Collection pack with three etched cards from the anniversary subset, including the Base Set Charizard reprint. Then the promos: a foil Pikachu ex illustrated by YOSHIROTTEN in a day or night version, and a foil Espeon ex or Umbreon ex depending on the box. A playmat, deck box, 65 sleeves, a metal coin and six damage-counter dice round it out.

The two versions are identical except for the theme — the featured Eeveelution, the Pikachu art, and the accessories. Espeon and Umbreon are the Gen II Eeveelutions, the pair that has anchored Pokémon collecting since Gold and Silver, and the 30th anniversary is the first time they have headlined a premium box together.

The market

The resale market did what it always does with a premium Eeveelution product: it went vertical, then started coming back down.

TCGplayer's pre-release market figures put both versions at more than three times retail — roughly 3.1 times for Day and 3.3 times for Night. At launch, market tracking had both boxes above $420. But the peak is already behind us: in the week before launch, Night fell 25% and Day fell 22.6% from their highs, as supply actually reached shelves.

Good luck choosing, though. Best Buy listed the collections with a one-per-order restriction and assigns buyers a random Day or Night version rather than letting them pick. In France, only Night released on November 6 — Day is expected November 27.

Why it matters

The 30th Celebration set itself launched September 16 — all-foil cards, 30 unique Pikachu cards with one guaranteed in every booster, a new Futuristic Rare rarity. The Day & Night boxes are the anniversary's premium capstone, and the Classic Collection pack inside is the only place to pull those gold-bordered reprints of three decades of icons.

The market cooling off a pre-release spike is normal. The question is where the floor is: at $179.99 retail with 30 packs and two exclusive promos, the box is fairly priced even before resale. If you can find one at retail, that is the buy. If you are paying $420, you are paying for the Eeveelutions, not the cardboard.

Gaming · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:55 AM ET

Resident Evil Requiem takes Game of the Year at the Golden Joysticks — and a two-person indie matched it

By Matt

Capcom's Resident Evil Requiem won Ultimate Game of the Year at the 44th Golden Joystick Awards in London. The night's other story: Meccha Chameleon, made by two people in five months, won four awards — the most ever by an indie at the ceremony.

The night

The 44th Golden Joystick Awards took over the Troxy in London on Wednesday night, and the headline result went the way most people expected: Resident Evil Requiem is the Ultimate Game of the Year.

Capcom's survival-horror sequel did not just win the big one. It took Console Game of the Year, Best Game Trailer and Best Lead Performer for Angela Sant'Albano as Grace Ashcroft — four awards for one game, and seven for Capcom overall, including Studio of the Year. PRAGMATA and Onimusha: Way of the Sword added to the haul. Requiem has now sold 8.5 million copies.

The rest of the major prizes spread out: Crimson Desert won PC Game of the Year, The Witcher 4 took Most Wanted Game, CONTROL Resonant won Best Visual Design and Onimusha took Best Audio Design. The show also premiered the announcement trailer for Conan: The Black Pyramid, a roguelite action RPG with two-player online co-op, planned for PC in 2027.

The other winner

The best story of the night was not the favorite winning. It was the tie.

Meccha Chameleon — a hide-and-seek party game made by two people, Lemorion and Haganeiro, in roughly five months — also won four awards, matching Requiem for the most on the night. It beat Marathon, Valheim and ARC Raiders for Best Multiplayer, topped Mewgenics and Cairn for Best Self-Published Indie, and won the Developers' Choice award over Mortal Shell 2 and Orbitals. It won every category it was nominated in except Best PC Game, which went to Crimson Desert.

Those four wins are the most any indie game has ever taken home from a single Golden Joystick ceremony in the awards' 44-year history. The game has sold more than 20 million copies — more than Pokémon Legends Z-A, God of War Ragnarök and Resident Evil Village managed in their lifetimes.

One more record fell on the night: with its Best Remake/Remaster win, The Witcher 3 now has 10 Golden Joystick wins in total, the most of any game in the ceremony's history.

The fine print

The Golden Joysticks run early in the fall, and the cutoff shows: November releases like the Ocarina of Time remake and Grand Theft Auto VI were not eligible for Ultimate Game of the Year. The Game Awards, the other big show of the season, is still to come — and those two games will be very much in that conversation.

Sports · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

Kucherov's natural hat trick buries Buffalo — and Carlson hits 800 points on the same play

By Matt

Nikita Kucherov scored a natural hat trick in the third period as Tampa Bay beat Buffalo 8-3. John Carlson had five points in the win, and his assist on Kucherov's third goal was the 800th point of his career.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Tampa Bay Lightning led Buffalo 4-0 midway through the second period on Wednesday night, and then nearly gave the whole thing away. The Sabres scored three straight — Josh Doan and Jason Zucker late in the second, Tage Thompson 33 seconds into the third — and a blowout was suddenly a one-goal game at KeyBank Center.

Then Nikita Kucherov did what he does.

Fifty-two seconds after Thompson's goal, Kucherov went in alone on a breakaway and beat Ukko-Pekka Luukkonen with a backhand under his left arm to make it 5-3 — ending Luukkonen's night in the process. At 4:11 he one-timed a John Carlson feed from the right circle to make it 6-3. At 14:13, from the same spot, on the power play, off another Carlson feed, he completed the natural hat trick: three goals, one period, no one else scoring in between. Brandon Hagel added his second of the night at 15:06 for the 8-3 final.

Kucherov finished with three goals and an assist. Carlson finished with a goal and four assists — five points — and the assist on the hat-trick goal was the 800th point of his NHL career. Jake Guentzel added four assists of his own, Anthony Cirelli had three, and Andrei Vasilevskiy made 28 saves. Tampa Bay improved to 12-4-2 and is 5-1-2 in its last eight. Buffalo fell to 8-7-3, losers of three of four.

The history

The natural hat trick was the third of Kucherov's career — the most in Lightning history. It was his eighth hat trick overall, tying Martin St. Louis for the second-most in franchise history. Steven Stamkos, with 13, is the only name ahead of him.

Carlson's milestone landed on the same play, which is fitting: 800 career points (176 goals, 624 assists) for a defenseman who has spent his whole career setting up exactly that kind of goal. "Nothing he does surprises me now," Carlson said of Kucherov afterward. Coach Jon Cooper was blunter: "'Kuch' did a Kuch thing."

It is worth remembering where this story was a week ago. Kucherov scored the overtime winner against Minnesota on Sunday, seven days after his trade request became public — and then made it clear, again, that he intends to keep playing for the Lightning. A natural hat trick in a game his team nearly let slip is the loudest possible version of that message.

The fine print

Two footnotes from a wild night in Buffalo: rookie Sam O'Reilly, making his NHL debut, picked up his first career point with the secondary assist on Yanni Gourde's goal. And defenseman Erik Cernak left the game in the second period after colliding with the curved glass — Cooper said he is "probably out tomorrow" when Tampa Bay visits Columbus on Thursday. Cirelli played his 600th NHL game.

Sports · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

Macklin Celebrini is the fifth-youngest player ever to 200 points — and he did it in a loss

By Matt

Macklin Celebrini scored his 200th career point at 20 years and 151 days old — fifth-youngest in NHL history, behind only Crosby, Gretzky, Carson and Lemieux — then added his 201st in the same game.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The San Jose Sharks lost 3-2 in a shootout to the Pittsburgh Penguins on Wednesday night at SAP Center, blowing a game they had clawed back from 2-0 down in the third period. Ben Kindel scored in regulation and had the lone shootout goal for Pittsburgh. Sergei Murashov made 34 saves for the Penguins.

But the night belonged to the losing captain anyway.

With just over eight minutes left in the third, Macklin Celebrini broke up Murashov's shutout, pulling San Jose within one. That goal was the 200th point of his NHL career, in his 171st game. Four minutes later he added his 201st, the primary assist on Mason Marchment's tying goal — Marchment's 100th career goal.

The history

At 20 years and 151 days old, Celebrini is the fifth-youngest player in NHL history to reach 200 career points. The four names ahead of him are the entire argument for why this matters: Sidney Crosby (19 years, 207 days), Wayne Gretzky (19 years, 347 days), Jimmy Carson (20 years, 107 days) and Mario Lemieux (20 years, 133 days).

He is also just the 14th player ever to pass the 200-point mark before turning 21, and the fifth-fastest active player to get there. Within his own franchise, the pace is unmatched — no player who began his career with the Sharks has ever reached 200 points this fast. The previous franchise mark belonged to Logan Couture, who needed 273 games. Celebrini did it in 171.

The context around him makes it louder. Celebrini is the No. 1 pick of the 2024 draft, the youngest captain in Sharks history and the sixth-youngest captain in NHL history. In July he signed a five-year, $94 million extension — $18.8 million a year — starting in 2027-28. San Jose has already decided what he is. Nights like this one are the receipt.

What's next

The Sharks hit the road Saturday at Utah. The milestone watch is over; the next one is 300, and at this pace it is not that far away.

Sports · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

The Warriors' win streak is over — and Curry left after six minutes

By Matt

Houston beat Golden State 115-108 on Wednesday, ending the Warriors' eight-game win streak. Stephen Curry played six minutes before leaving with right abdominal soreness, and the MRI results weren't known after the game.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Houston Rockets beat the Golden State Warriors 115-108 on Wednesday night in Houston, and the final score only tells half of it. The Warriors' eight-game winning streak — the run that started after an 0-3 opening and made them the first team ever to open a season with three losses and then win eight in a row — is over.

The bigger worry left the floor in the first quarter. Stephen Curry played six minutes before departing with right abdominal soreness. He underwent an MRI after the game, and the results weren't immediately known, coach Steve Kerr said postgame. Golden State played most of the night without the player carrying the streak — Curry was averaging 30.6 points a game, 32.3 over his last five.

Houston did not need a masterpiece to close it out. Alperen Sengun flirted with a triple-double — 19 points, 13 rebounds, nine assists — Kevin Durant led the Rockets with 20, and Amen Thompson added 19 points and seven rebounds. The Rockets pulled away in the fourth quarter for their second straight win, improving to 9-3. Golden State falls to 8-4.

Why it matters

Streaks end. That is the boring part. The interesting part is how this one ended — not with a cold shooting night or a buzzer-beater, but with the best player on the floor walking to the locker room six minutes in and the team holding on for three quarters before Houston's depth took over.

The Warriors have been shorthanded all through the run — Jimmy Butler, Moses Moody and Gary Payton II are all out — and they kept winning anyway. But there is shorthanded and then there is no Curry at all, and Wednesday was the second one. Until the MRI results come back, the streak is the smaller story. The abdominal is the story.

Around the league

Boston pulled off its own 22-point comeback on the same night, beating Toronto 98-93 behind Jayson Tatum's 24 — the Celtics' fifth straight win. James Harden scored a season-high 32 as Cleveland won 115-107 at the Garden.

Sports · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

Spurs win their 10th straight after erasing a 22-point hole in Chicago

By Matt

Victor Wembanyama scored 32 and Dylan Harper added 18 points, 10 assists and four steals as the Spurs came back from 22 down to beat the Bulls 124-119 — their 10th straight win after an 0-2 start.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The San Antonio Spurs trailed by 22 points midway through the second quarter in Chicago on Wednesday night. The Bulls were 10-for-12 from three, the United Center was loud, and the Spurs looked like a team about to take its first loss in two weeks.

Then they did what they have done all month: they won anyway.

Victor Wembanyama scored 32 points and Dylan Harper — 18 points, 10 assists, four steals — ran the comeback as San Antonio beat the Bulls 124-119, closing out a 4-0 road trip. It was the second night in a row the Spurs had to dig out of a hole, and the second night in a row Wembanyama delivered: he dropped 40 with nine blocks in 25 minutes against Phoenix two days earlier.

The Bulls led 64-42 before the Spurs started chipping away, and Chicago's collapse is its own story. The Bulls have now lost eight straight to fall to 1-9, winless in four home games. Their biggest lead of the season lasted about a quarter.

The history

The record book cares about how the run started, not just how long it has gone. San Antonio opened the season 0-2. Since then, ten wins in a row — and that makes the Spurs just the second team in NBA history to start 0-2 and then win ten straight. The only other club to do it: the 2017-18 Boston Celtics, who turned an 0-2 start into a 16-2 stretch.

That is a very different kind of streak than an undefeated start. Nobody remembers the two losses in October because the response has been perfect since. Ten in a row, and the last one came from 22 down on the road.

What's next

The test gets harder immediately. Golden State comes to San Antonio on Friday night — the same Warriors team that just saw its own eight-game win streak snapped in Houston, and the same team the Spurs beat to start this run's momentum. A win there makes it eleven, and the conversation changes from "nice streak" to "how far can this go?"

Sports · Nov 12, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

McDavid's point streak hits 20 — only the fourth player ever to open a season like this

By Matt

Connor McDavid picked up an assist in the Oilers' 3-2 overtime win over the Flyers, running his season-opening point streak to 20 games. Only Gretzky, Sundin and Heatley have ever done that.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Edmonton Oilers needed overtime to beat the Philadelphia Flyers 3-2 at Rogers Place on Wednesday, and the game-winner came 47 seconds into the extra session — Evan Bouchard one-timing a feed from Leon Draisaitl short side, off the right post and in. It was Bouchard's 11th goal of the season, the most by any NHL defenseman, and his second overtime winner of the year.

The goal that mattered most for the history books came earlier. Midway through the second period, Connor McDavid found Zach Hyman posted in front of the net on the power play, and Hyman redirected it through Dan Vladar's legs for his second goal of the night. The assist ran the captain's season-opening point streak to 20 games.

Hyman's first multi-goal game of the season could not have come at a better time — he entered the night with one goal all year. Tristan Jarry made 30 saves, and the Oilers improved to 14-3-3, matching the second-best start in franchise history through 20 games. They have won three straight and are 12-0-2 when they score first.

Philadelphia, for its part, did not roll over. The Flyers erased a two-goal deficit on goals from Tyson Foerster and Travis Sanheim to force overtime, and they outshot Edmonton 32-24. They fell to 5-10-6, losers of four of their last five.

The history

Twenty games is where this streak stops being a hot start and becomes a line in the record book. McDavid is just the fourth player in NHL history to record a point in each of his team's first 20 games of a season. The other three: Wayne Gretzky (three times, including the record 51-game run to open 1983-84), Mats Sundin (30 games in 1992-93) and Dany Heatley (22 games in 2005-06).

It is also the second 20-game streak of McDavid's own career — he had one last season, too — making him the eighth player ever with multiple 20-game point streaks. The 20-game mark ties his career high.

The scoring lead is not close anymore. The captain has 49 points — 11 goals and 38 assists — through 20 games, 14 clear of Minnesota's Kirill Kaprizov. For context, he had more points than the Flyers had total goals (45) before the puck dropped on Wednesday.

What's next

The next stop is 21, then Heatley's 22. Sundin's 30 is the realistic horizon; Gretzky's 51 is a different sport. Edmonton starts a six-game road trip Saturday in Toronto, then faces these same Flyers again Sunday in Philadelphia.

MLB · Nov 11, 2026 · 8:43 PM ET

Schlittler and Misiorowski win Cy Young Awards unanimously — youngest duo in 41 years

By Matt

Cam Schlittler and Jacob Misiorowski both swept all 30 first-place votes, the youngest Cy Young-winning pair since 1985 — and Misiorowski adds the award to a World Series title in the same season.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The announcement

The BBWAA announced the 2026 Cy Young Award winners on Wednesday night, and the vote wasn't close in either league. Yankees right-hander Cam Schlittler took the American League honor and Brewers ace Jacob Misiorowski took the National League honor — both unanimously, with all 30 first-place votes and 210 points apiece.

It's only the third time in history that both Cy Young winners were unanimous in the same year, after 1968 and 2022.

Cam Schlittler, AL Cy Young

Schlittler put together the best pitching season in the American League: 14-6 with a 1.95 ERA, 239 strikeouts and a 0.92 WHIP over 193.2 innings. His ERA led the AL, and his 7.0 WAR and 215 ERA+ led AL starters too.

He's the 13th unanimous AL Cy Young winner, and the seventh Yankee to win the award — the first since Gerrit Cole in 2023. Dylan Cease finished second with 110 points and Parker Messick third with 84.

Jacob Misiorowski, NL Cy Young

Misiorowski was just as dominant on the other side: 16-5 with a 1.80 ERA — the best in the majors — plus 252 strikeouts and a 0.80 WHIP over 174.2 innings, with a 6.4 WAR across 30 starts.

He's the fourth Brewer ever to win the Cy Young, joining Rollie Fingers (1981), Pete Vukovich (1982) and Corbin Burnes (2021). Cristopher Sánchez finished second with 99 points, one ahead of Chris Sale's 98.

The history

The two winners are 24 and 25 — the youngest Cy Young-winning duo since 1985, when 20-year-old Dwight Gooden and 21-year-old Bret Saberhagen swept the awards.

Both are second-year arms in their first full seasons after midseason call-ups in 2025, and Misiorowski did it with the loudest stuff in the game: he threw 28 of the 35 hardest pitches in the majors this season, ranging from 104 to 105.5 mph.

Misiorowski also joins Randy Johnson (2001) and Justin Verlander (2022) as the only pitchers since 2000 to win the Cy Young and the World Series in the same year, after helping the Brewers to their first title.

Also tonight

Mason Miller was unanimously chosen as the National League Reliever of the Year, and Louis Varland took the American League honor.

What's next

Awards week ends Thursday night with the MVPs. Pete Crow-Armstrong is the favorite in the National League and Yordan Alvarez in the American League — but the winners aren't official until the announcement.

Red Sox ready to let Sonny Gray walk in free agency

By Matt

Boston is comfortable letting Sonny Gray walk, per Alex Speier — Craig Breslow calls the rotation a strength for 2027, so the money goes to position players instead.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The call

The Red Sox are comfortable letting Sonny Gray walk in free agency, according to Alex Speier on the 310 to Left podcast. Craig Breslow called the rotation a strength for 2027, so Boston plans to put that money toward position players instead.

It's a clear-eyed call, not a shrug. Gray was one of Boston's best arms this year, but the front office is betting the rotation holds up without him — and that the lineup is where the next dollars matter more.

What Gray did in Boston

Gray's one season in a Red Sox uniform was a strong one: 18-5 with a 2.72 ERA and 149 strikeouts over 169 innings in 29 starts. Boston declined his $30 million mutual option for 2027, and he took the $10 million buyout into the open market instead.

Record: 18-5

ERA: 2.72

Strikeouts: 149

Innings: 169

Starts: 29

That line will make him one of the more attractive arms available this winter — and it also explains why Boston isn't chasing him. A pitcher coming off a season like that doesn't come cheap, and the Sox have decided the rotation is already covered.

Where Boston goes from here

The question now is who fills the innings. Boston has already been linked to Freddy Peralta as a possible replacement — The Stat Line covered the $92 million prediction earlier this week — but the message from Breslow is that the front office isn't panicking about the rotation. The priority is the lineup.

That tracks with the rest of Boston's offseason so far. The Sox have already signaled they're open to moving Jarren Duran and Masataka Yoshida, with the outfield set around Roman Anthony, Wilyer Abreu and Ceddanne Rafaela. The money that would have gone to Gray now looks earmarked for bats.

TSL Bets: The Wednesday parlay (~+600) — Celtics -7.5, Lakers -11.5, Penguins-Sharks Over 6.5

By Matt

Three legs, three different bets: the Celtics covering in Toronto, the Lakers rolling a one-win Bucks team, and a goals total in San Jose. One big spread, one smaller one, one total — about +600.

Leg 1: Celtics -7.5 at Raptors

Boston Celtics (9-2) at Toronto Raptors (5-5) — 7:00 PM ET, Scotiabank Arena

Celtics -7.5 (-110), total 215.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Injuries — Boston: Nobody on the injury report. The rotation is fully healthy.

Injuries — Toronto: Scottie Barnes is out with a groin injury (third straight game), and Kawhi Leonard is out with a knee injury (out at least another week). Immanuel Quickley is questionable with a hamstring injury, and Collin Murray-Boyles and Trayce Jackson-Davis are questionable with illnesses.

Storylines: The Celtics have won four straight and are going for a fifth, after beating the 76ers 123-101 on Tuesday — Jaylen Brown scored 36 and Jayson Tatum added 21 points, 6 rebounds and 4 assists. Toronto is missing its two best players. The caveat is real: Boston is on the second night of a back-to-back, and there was a note earlier in the week that Brown might get a rest day on Wednesday — he played Tuesday after resting Monday, so the rest risk is worth naming. But the Raptors without Barnes and Kawhi are a different team, and the Celtics are healthy.

Leg 2: Lakers -11.5 at Bucks

Los Angeles Lakers (10-2) at Milwaukee Bucks (1-10) — 8:00 PM ET, Fiserv Forum

Lakers -11.5 (-110), total 232.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Injuries — Los Angeles: Austin Reaves is back — off the injury report after the illness that kept him out Monday. Luka Doncic is clear. Quentin Grimes is questionable with a right finger sprain.

Injuries — Milwaukee: Tyler Herro is out with a right knee contusion — and he is the Bucks' leading scorer at 21.4 points a game.

Storylines: The Bucks are 1-10. They have lost six straight, and they lost to the Knicks 117-85 on Tuesday. The Lakers are 10-2, on a six-game win streak, and they just won in Atlanta without Reaves. This is the parlay's one big spread — the Lakers are the only favorite laying more than 8.5 — and it is the leg with the most room to be wrong, because 11.5 points on the road is a big number. But the Bucks are missing their leading scorer, they are losing by double digits most nights, and the Lakers are the best team in the league right now.

Leg 3: Penguins at Sharks Over 6.5

Pittsburgh Penguins (6-10-2) at San Jose Sharks (8-11-1) — 10:00 PM ET, SAP Center

Penguins -125, total 6.5, Over -110 (DraftKings via ESPN). Projected goalies: Arturs Silovs (PIT) vs Yaroslav Askarov (SJ).

Injuries — Pittsburgh: Bryan Rust is on IR with a lower-body injury, Ryan Graves is on IR with a knee injury, and Elmer Soderblom is on IR. Ville Koivunen is day-to-day with a lower-body injury, and James van Riemsdyk is on IR.

Injuries — San Jose: Igor Chernyshov is on IR, Quentin Musty is on IR-NR, and Michael Keyser is on IR. All depth.

Storylines: Macklin Celebrini is at 199 career points in 170 games (77 goals, 122 assists) and has 21 points in 18 games this season — he took six shots on Monday against the Islanders and came away with no points, so he is due. The goalie matchup is two young netminders, Silovs and Askarov, and both teams leak goals — Pittsburgh is 29th in the NHL, San Jose 25th. Rudebets projects a 6.86 total for this game. The Over needs seven goals, and these two teams give up enough of them.

The bet

Parlay: Celtics -7.5 (-110) + Lakers -11.5 (-110) + Penguins-Sharks Over 6.5 (-110) — about +600.

One big spread, one smaller spread, one total. That is the whole point of the construction: the Lakers are the only leg laying more than 8.5, and the other two legs are built on different kinds of bets — a healthy favorite covering a manageable number, and a goals total between two leaky teams. None of these legs are copies of the single-game bets today, and each one clears on its own merits.

The honest caveat

The Lakers are the risk. Eleven and a half points is a big number on the road, and if the Bucks keep it close — or if the Lakers take their foot off the gas in the fourth — the whole ticket dies on the second leg. The Celtics are on a back-to-back with a rest risk on their best player, and the Over needs seven goals from two teams that can both go quiet. A parlay is a parlay: three things have to go right, and one of them is a double-digit road spread. Bet accordingly.

The pick

Celtics -7.5 + Lakers -11.5 + Penguins-Sharks Over 6.5 — about +600. A $10 ticket returns about $70. One big spread, one smaller one, one total — three different bets, one ticket.

But the Oilers are the better team by a wide margin, and the price is right.

The pick

Oilers -1.5 (+124). A $10 ticket returns about $22.40. The best offense in hockey, at home, against a 5-10-5 team, at plus money.

Collectibles · Nov 11, 2026 · 7:57 AM ET

Burger King is selling a Magic: The Gathering deck box with your Whopper — Italy only

By Matt

Burger King Italy has launched 'The Great Gathering' — a €19.90 bundle that comes with a rigid burger-styled Magic deck box and 100 sleeves. No promo cards, but nine restaurants are running Pauper tournaments with a final at Milan Games Week.

The bundle

Magic: The Gathering has done a lot of crossovers. A fast-food one was not on the list until this week.

Burger King Italy has launched "Burger King Quest: The Great Gathering," a bundle that runs from November 10 through November 30 at participating Italian locations. The price is €19.90 — about $22 — and it gets you a choice of sandwich, fries and a drink, plus the thing everyone is actually here for: a rigid Burger King x Magic deck box, styled to look like a burger, and a pack of 100 card sleeves.

The sandwich is your choice of the Chicken Krispper, the Double Cheese King, or a plant-based Whopper. The box itself is the prize — an orange, sesame-seed-dotted deck box with the Burger King logo on one side and the Magic logo on the other — and Polygon reports it is already being resold online, with scalpers flipping the box before the promotion is even halfway through.

There are no promo cards in the bundle. The accessories are the product.

The tournaments

This is not just a meal with a toy in it. Nine Burger King restaurants across Italy are hosting Pauper tournaments as part of the promotion — competitive Magic in the cheap, commons-only format — with qualifiers running through the month and a final at Milan Games Week & Cartoomics, November 27–29.

It is a real competitive structure wrapped around a fast-food promotion, which is exactly why it is working. The Pauper community gets a tournament circuit with a national final; Burger King gets a month of people coming through the door for something they cannot get at a card shop.

Why it matters

Wizards of the Coast has never done a fast-food partnership like this. The closest comparison is Pokémon's Happy Meal runs, and those move staggering numbers — the recent U.S. Pokémon Happy Meal launch had McDonald's locations selling out within days. Burger King is betting the same energy works for an older, tournament-playing audience, and the resale market says they were right to try.

The math is worth noting, too. A regular Burger King meal in Italy runs around €11.50. The bundle at €19.90 means the deck box and sleeves are effectively costing about €8.40 — less than most standalone deck boxes, and this one looks like a burger.

The catch, of course, is that it is Italy only. No U.S. dates, no wider European rollout announced. If you want one, you either know someone in Milan or you pay the reseller price — which, given how this promotion is going, is not going down.

Air Jordan 11 'Magic Ball' drops today — the Space Jam 30th anniversary women's exclusive, $235

By Elijah

The Air Jordan 11 'Magic Ball' drops today, November 11 — women's sizes only, $235, via Nike SNKRS, Nike.com and Foot Locker. It's the first drop in the Space Jam 30th Anniversary Collection, inspired by the ball the Monstars used to steal the NBA's talent.

The drop

The Air Jordan 11 "Magic Ball" drops today, Wednesday, November 11 — women's sizes only, $235, available through Nike SNKRS, Nike.com and Foot Locker, plus select retailers.

It is the first release in the Space Jam 30th Anniversary Collection, and the concept is as fun as anything Jordan Brand has done in a while: the shoe is inspired by the magic ball the Monstars used to steal the NBA players' talents in Space Jam. The iridescent patent leather — gold shifting to green depending on the light — is the visual version of that idea, with black laces and an icy outsole to finish it.

Why this one

The 11 is the Space Jam shoe. The original "Concord" is the sneaker Michael Jordan wore in the film, and every anniversary cycle since has used the 11 to anchor the collection. This year's slate has the "Magic Ball" today, the "Green Screen" on November 14, and the main "Space Jam Galaxy" course after that.

The "Magic Ball" is also the most interesting of the three on its own terms. It is not a retro reissue of a colorway everyone already owns — it is a new idea, a women's exclusive, and it lands on Singles' Day, November 11, which has become one of the biggest sneaker dates of the year. The combination of a fresh concept and a limited sizing run is exactly what makes a release like this sell through.

Where to get it

Nike SNKRS is the first stop, with Nike.com and Foot Locker carrying it as well. Women's sizing only — if you wear men's sizes, this one is not for you, but the rest of the Space Jam slate will have wider runs.

Ubisoft makes it official: the Tubarão-Noor combo is banned for the rest of the Osaka Major

By Matt

Ubisoft has ruled on the Rainbow Six Siege combo that dominated the BLAST R6 Major Osaka's opening days: the Tubarão-Noor gadget interaction is banned until the end of the event, and any team that uses it loses the round.

The ruling

The BLAST R6 Major Osaka had a problem before it really got started, and on Wednesday Ubisoft finally put it in writing.

The interaction between Tubarão's Zoto Canister and Noor's Horus Lance is banned from now until the end of the event. Any team confirmed using it loses the round. The ruling is not retroactive — results from the opening days stand — and neither operator is banned. Tubarão and Noor are still playable; their gadgets just cannot be used together anymore.

The ruling comes under section 4.6 of the Osaka Major rulebook, and it closes a saga that began the moment the tournament opened.

How it worked

Here is the combo: Tubarão's Zoto Canister freezes both a Horus Lance and a hard breach gadget. When the freeze ends, the Lance disables the gadget it was touching. The result is a hard-breach denial that is, on paper, perfectly legal — and brutally effective.

The players knew what it was the second they saw it. On the tournament's opening day, teams raised it with BLAST, and BLAST confirmed with Ubisoft that the interaction was intentional — not a bug. That is where the trouble started. If Ubisoft knew the interaction existed, the players wanted to know why nobody said anything before the Major began. The patch notes said nothing. The teams found out on stage.

The gentlemen's agreement

Four days ago, the players took it into their own hands. All twenty teams at the Major agreed — a voluntary gentlemen's agreement, announced publicly by Team Liquid in-game leader Felipe "FelipoX" Lucia on November 7 — not to use the interaction at all. It was, by most accounts, the first agreement of its kind in Rainbow Six Siege esports in years.

Now the agreement is a rule. Ubisoft's official ruling, published Wednesday, matches the players' position almost exactly: banned from November 11 until the end of the event, round loss for confirmed use, operators still playable separately.

It is a strange way for a major to be decided — the players agreed on the competitive integrity of the tournament before the publisher did — but the important part is that the question is settled before the weekend. The grand final is this weekend, and the Season 4 update gets its reveal there too. The players can finally go back to playing the game instead of arguing about it.

Kai Cenat says he's 'genuinely terrified of human beings' as the Clover Boys fallout spills over

By Matt

Dezz says Kai Cenat cut off the Clover Boys. Kai answered on his GTA marathon stream — then admitted on camera that he's 'so scared to meet new people.' Here's what we actually know.

What happened

The Clover Boys are the group Kai Cenat built — Dezz, Tylil, Punga, RaKai, Ray and Reggie, a streaming circle that goes back to college. This week, one of its founders said it was falling apart in public.

Dezz hosted a TikTok livestream on November 9 and accused Kai of cutting off communication with him and the other Clover Boys streamers. His line, as it made the rounds: Kai "don't speak to none of us gang." He also said Kaiya Cenat — Kai's sister — had stopped talking to him as well.

Kai addressed it the same day, during his GTA marathon stream. He was not having it. He told his chat he was "tired of the bull**" and that people should "leave him alone" — the kind of answer that says more by what it does not say than by what it does.

Then, on November 10, a different clip started circulating — shared by X user @scubaryan_ — where Kai talked about why he pulls back from people at all. "I'm genuinely terrified of human beings," he said. "I'm so scared to meet new people." It was not a response to Dezz, exactly. It was the context underneath the whole thing.

Why this one stings

Kai and Dezz go back further than most people in this scene. They met in college, and Dezz helped produce Kai's early videos — he was there before the fame, before the numbers, before the marathon streams. The Clover Boys were supposed to be the answer to the lonely-at-the-top problem: a group that grew together instead of one star outgrowing everyone around him.

But the fractures have been visible for a while. Reggie distanced himself from the group in September, and Kai said in October that he has invested $3.7 million into the Clover Boys. When the money and the distance both get that big, a group of friends starts to look like a business — and businesses have arguments that friendships do not survive.

What we don't know

The important part: neither Kai nor Kaiya has confirmed Dezz's account of being cut off, and the full picture is murkier than one livestream makes it sound. Kai streamed with Ray in Japan recently, which cuts against the idea that he has shut everyone out. The "cut off everyone" claim, in other words, is one person's telling of it — and Kai's own words on stream read less like a denial of the distance and more like an explanation of why it exists.

This is not a story with a villain. It is a story about a streamer who got famous faster than anyone in his circle expected, and a group that is finding out what that does to a friendship.

Dawson Mercer burns his old team as Jets beat Devils 4-2 in Hellebuyck's return to Winnipeg

By Matt

Dawson Mercer scored twice against the team that traded him — including the power-play winner — and Nico Daws got the win in the other net as Winnipeg beat New Jersey 4-2 on the night Connor Hellebuyck came home and never played.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Tuesday in Winnipeg was supposed to be about Connor Hellebuyck. The former Jets goalie came back to Canada Life Centre for the first time since his trade request was granted on October 23, and the building was ready for him — the fans booed him like he had started all 60 minutes.

He never played a second. The Devils went with Jake Allen in net, and the two players New Jersey sent the other way in the deal decided the game instead.

Dawson Mercer scored twice against his former team, including the tiebreaking power-play goal with 8:02 left in the third period, and Nico Daws — the other half of the return package — made 19 saves for his first win as a Jet as Winnipeg beat New Jersey 4-2. Kyle Connor scored twice as well, adding an empty-netter with 58 seconds left to seal it.

The goals told the story of the night. Connor put Winnipeg up 2-0 in the first. Jesper Bratt and Cody Glass answered 44 seconds apart early in the second to tie it, and the building went quiet for a while. Then the Devils took a too-many-men penalty in the third, Mercer buried the power play at 11:58, and the Jets had their fourth straight win — this one after a nine-game skid that had buried their season before it started.

The trade, in one night

This is what a revenge game is supposed to look like. Mercer and Daws were not bit players in the Hellebuyck deal — they were the package — and on the night Hellebuyck returned to Winnipeg, they were the two best Jets on the ice. Mercer has now scored three goals since changing teams. Daws outdueled Allen, who made 21 saves in the loss.

Hellebuyck, for his part, watched from the bench as the crowd chanted at him. Devils general manager Sunny Mehta had announced on Sunday that Hellebuyck would not start, and head coach Scott Arniel said afterward that he hopes the win brings closure for his team — the Jets still have to go to New Jersey on November 28, but the home half of the saga is done.

The loss ended New Jersey's five-game winning streak and dropped the Devils to 13-6-0. Winnipeg improved to 8-8-3, and after nine straight losses, four straight wins has them back in the conversation.

The bigger picture

The Jets spent the first month of the season in a hole of their own making. Four straight wins does not erase a nine-game skid, but beating the team that took your franchise goalie — with the two players you got back doing the damage — is the kind of night that changes how a room feels about itself.

Winnipeg visits Dallas on Thursday. New Jersey hosts Washington the same night.

Luka Doncic returns with 32, 8 and 10 as Lakers hold off Hawks for sixth straight win

By Matt

Luka Doncic came back from a one-game absence with 32 points, 8 rebounds and 10 assists as the Lakers beat the Hawks 124-114 in Atlanta — their sixth straight win, even with Austin Reaves sidelined.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Luka Doncic missed one game. The Lakers did not miss a beat.

Back in the lineup after sitting out Sunday in Detroit with lower-back tightness, Doncic put up 32 points, 8 rebounds, 10 assists, 5 steals and 2 blocks in 39 minutes as Los Angeles beat the Atlanta Hawks 124-114 at State Farm Arena on Monday night. The win is the Lakers' sixth in a row, and it pushes them to 10-2 — the best record in the league.

He did it the way he has been doing it all season: everywhere. Doncic shot 11-of-22 from the field and 7-of-7 from the line, had 8 assists before halftime, and scored 18 of his points after the break as Atlanta kept charging. The Hawks cut a double-digit lead to 115-111 with about three and a half minutes left, and the Lakers still closed it out — their bench doing most of the closing.

That bench was the story of the night. With Austin Reaves out with an illness one night after his season-high 43 in Detroit, the Lakers' reserves scored a season-high 49 points. Sandro Mamukelashvili led the way with 23 points, 8 rebounds and 3 assists in his best game as a Laker. A night after the bench put up 45 in Detroit without Doncic, it put up 49 without Reaves. Coach JJ Redick praised the depth afterward, and he had every reason to.

The streak ends

The one thing Doncic did not do: score 40. His run of four straight 40-point games ended in Atlanta, eight points short of a fifth — the number that would have put him alongside Wilt Chamberlain, Kobe Bryant, Michael Jordan, Allen Iverson, Elgin Baylor and James Harden as the only players in NBA history with five consecutive 40-point games.

He will live with it. The Lakers are 10-2, he still leads the league in scoring at just over 39 points a game, and he was named Western Conference Player of the Week on Monday after averaging 45.0 points, 7.0 rebounds and 8.3 assists across a 3-0 week. The scoring streak was fun. The winning streak matters more, and it is still alive.

Atlanta, meanwhile, saw its own four-game winning streak end. The Hawks had a real chance late and could not finish, and now they watch the Lakers head to Milwaukee on Wednesday with Reaves expected back and Quentin Grimes — out Monday with a sprained right finger — listed as questionable.

The draft-night troll

There was one more moment worth keeping. Doncic was drafted third overall in 2018 by Atlanta, then traded on draft night to Dallas for Trae Young and a protected first-round pick. On Monday, with the game in hand, he had words with a Hawks fan in the crowd — the clip made the rounds within the hour — needling the home fans about the team that drafted him and sent him away.

Eight years later, the trade is still one of the most second-guessed deals in modern NBA history, and Doncic is still happy to remind Atlanta of it in person.

Celtics dominate 76ers in first Tatum-Brown meeting as Paul George torches his old team

By Matt

The summer trade's grudge match went to Boston — and the new guy in green stole the show.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The game everyone had circled since the summer ended the way Boston drew it up. The Celtics beat the 76ers 123-101 in Philadelphia on Tuesday night, in the first regular-season meeting since the blockbuster that sent Jaylen Brown to the Sixers and Paul George to Boston — and they never trailed. The headliner was supposed to be Jayson Tatum against his former running mate. The story turned out to be the other guy in the trade.

The night belonged to George

George went off against the team that dealt him away, pouring in a game-high 36 points on 14-of-22 shooting, including 6-of-11 from three, with 5 rebounds. He did his loudest damage late: the broadcast graphic during the run said it plainly — an 18-3 Celtics burst over 5:21 that turned a game into a rout. Every time Philadelphia threatened to make it interesting, the ball found George, and the ball went in.

This was the revenge-game script in its purest form. George spent the summer as the footnote to the Brown-Tatum storyline, the other piece of the deal. In his first game back in Philadelphia wearing green, he was the best player on the floor by a distance, smiling all the way through it. He'd told reporters before shootaround that it feels like "a regular game." It did not look like one.

Round one: Tatum over Brown

Tatum got his win over his old running mate, finishing with 21 points, 6 rebounds, and 4 assists, plus a third-quarter poster dunk on LeBron James that had the Boston bench on its feet. The two spent nine seasons together in Boston, won a championship together, and became the identity of the franchise — then the trade split them up, and the week leading in had all the makings of a feud. The scoreboard backed Tatum up.

Brown's first game against his old team was a quiet one: 13 points on 4-of-9 shooting, 5 rebounds, 2 assists, 4 turnovers, and a minus-27 in 26 minutes. He never found a rhythm, and by the fourth quarter he was watching from the bench as the lead stretched past 20. The building that used to cheer George spent the night watching him cook his old team.

Philly's side of the ledger

Tyrese Maxey kept the Sixers in it as long as anyone could, leading Philadelphia with 24 points on 8-of-12 shooting. Joel Embiid added 16, but needed 13 trips to the line to get there on a 2-of-9 night from the field. And LeBron James finished two boards and five dimes short of a triple-double, closing on 11 points, 8 rebounds, and 5 assists — the watch that had some life in the fourth quarter never got there.

Boston's bench buried them

The starters set the tone, but the bench put the game away. Payton Pritchard came off the bench for 18 points, 6 rebounds, and 5 assists and was a plus-26; Sam Hauser knocked down four threes for 12; Hugo Gonzalez added 10. Philadelphia's best stretch was the third quarter, the only frame it won — and even that only trimmed two points off a double-digit halftime deficit. Boston won the other three quarters going away.

What it means

The winner was always going to take sole possession of first place in the East, and now that belongs to Boston alone: the Celtics sit at 9-2, the Sixers at 9-3. It is one game in November, and the standings will move a dozen times before spring — but the trade's grudge match was not close out of the gate, and the team that supposedly lost its second star looks better for the swap so far. Boston doesn't wait long to build on it: the Celtics are in Toronto tomorrow night, Wednesday, November 11, at 7:00 PM ET against the Raptors. The rematch with Philly isn't until Jan. 21, at TD Garden.

ARC Raiders passes 17 million copies sold

By Matt

ARC Raiders has sold more than 17 million copies, Nexon said in its third-quarter earnings release — up from 16.3 million at the end of June, with the October Frozen Trail update pulling lapsed players back a full year after launch.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

ARC Raiders has sold more than 17 million copies. Nexon put the total in its third-quarter earnings release on November 10, 2026, and credited the October Frozen Trail update for pulling lapsed players back a full year after launch.

The numbers

Copies sold to date: More than 17 million

Copies sold at the end of June 2026: 16.3 million

Copies sold by May 2026: 16 million

Matches played in year one: Six billion

Average raids per day in year one: 16 million

Frozen Trail did the work

Nexon's earnings letter calls Frozen Trail, released by Embark Studios on October 8, "the largest update since the 2025 launch" — and says it was "successful in re-engaging lapsed players and driving incremental unit sales." That is the story behind the new total: the game did not just hold its audience, it won back players who had drifted away and sold new copies to players coming in fresh.

The pace tells the rest of it. The gap between the May figure and this one is roughly a million copies over about six months, after the launch rush did the heavy lifting in the first seven. Nexon also said third-quarter revenue from the game came in within its outlook, and that it expects fourth-quarter revenue to stay broadly flat against the third quarter while falling year over year — an honest comparison, since the same quarter last year was launch season.

What a year looks like

The game launched October 30, 2025, on PC, PlayStation 5, and Xbox Series X|S. By its first anniversary it had six billion matches played — an average of 16 million raids a day — and Nexon said in August it had generated more than 88 billion yen since launch. It is a buy-to-play game, so every active player is a paying player, which makes the retention story matter more than it would for a free-to-play title: nobody is padding the numbers with free downloads.

A year after launch, the question for an extraction shooter is never whether the launch was big. It is whether anyone is still raiding. Frozen Trail answered that, and the sales total is the receipt.

NHL · Nov 10, 2026 · 12:27 PM ET

The Chase, Ep. 1: Crosby is 24 points from tying Ron Francis for fifth all-time

By Matt

Sidney Crosby sits at 1,774 career points after passing Marcel Dionne on Saturday in Los Angeles. Ron Francis, fifth all-time at 1,798, is 24 points away from a tie — 25 from being passed. This is The Chase, and we're tracking it every step of the way.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The Chase is on

Sidney Crosby has 1,774 career points. Ron Francis, fifth on the NHL's all-time scoring list, has 1,798.

That's the whole story, and it's the only story that matters for the next few months: Crosby is 24 points from tying Francis, 25 from passing him. Every game from here is a step on that count, and this is the series that's going to track it — episode by episode, point by point, until the night he gets there.

Welcome to The Chase.

How he got here

Saturday night in Los Angeles, Crosby passed Marcel Dionne for sixth all-time. It wasn't a pretty goal — Evgeni Malkin's one-timer from the right circle caught a piece of Crosby on its way in at 17:13 of the second period, and the goal was first credited to Malkin before the replay showed the deflection. But it counted all the same: career point No. 1,772, past Dionne's 1,771, in the building where Dionne did most of his damage across 12 seasons as a King.

Crosby didn't know he'd been credited with it at first. He tried to steer the credit back to Malkin. That's the kind of night it was — and then, with 1:16 left in the third and the Kings' net empty, he dove to poke a loose puck home after being impeded by Erik Gustafsson on the breakaway. Point No. 1,773. Penguins 5, Kings 2. Four-game losing streak over, seven-game road skid over.

Sunday in Anaheim, he added one more: an assist in the Penguins' 5-2 loss to the Ducks. That makes it 1,774 — 660 goals and 1,114 assists in 1,438 games.

He tied Dionne at 1,771 in Montreal on October 31. It took him a week to pass him. He is 39 years old, in his 21st season, and he is still doing this.

The list ahead of him

The names left in front of Crosby are the ones on the building. Gretzky's 2,857 is untouchable, and Gordie Howe's 1,850 is a long way off yet. But Francis, at 1,798, is close enough to watch in real time.

Here's the math, straight off the pace: Crosby has 13 points in 18 games this season — 6 goals, 7 assists, 0.72 points per game. At that pace, the gap closes in roughly 33 games. There are 64 games left on the schedule. If he stays healthy and keeps producing anything like this, he doesn't just catch Francis this season — he clears him with room to spare.

That's pace math, not a prediction. Hockey doesn't work in straight lines, and a cold week or a missed game moves the date. But the direction is clear, and the gap is small enough that every point from here matters.

What it means

Crosby has been chasing history for so long that it's easy to forget how rare this is. Sixth all-time in scoring, in a league that's been around for more than a century, at 39, on a Penguins team that's rebuilding around him. Dionne held sixth place for decades. Crosby took it from him in a week.

And now there's one more name. Francis played 23 seasons and finished with 1,798 points. The next time the Penguins play, the count starts moving again.

What's next

The Penguins are in San Jose on Wednesday night — 10:00 PM ET against the Sharks at SAP Center — then back home Saturday at 7:00 PM ET against Ottawa. Every point Crosby picks up in those games comes off the gap.

We'll be here for all of it. The Chase continues Wednesday.

The box score

Final score (Nov 7): Penguins 5, Kings 2

Final score (Nov 8): Ducks 5, Penguins 2

Crosby: 1,774 career points (660 G, 1,114 A, 1,438 GP)

Milestone: Passed Marcel Dionne (1,771) for 6th all-time, Nov 7

Next target: Ron Francis, 1,798 (5th) — 24 to tie, 25 to pass

This season: 6 G, 7 A, 13 PTS in 18 GP (0.72 PPG)

Next game: Wed Nov 11, 10:00 PM ET at San Jose Sharks

Then: Sat Nov 14, 7:00 PM ET vs Ottawa Senators
