NFL · Nov 8, 2026 · 2:10 PM ET

Ja'Marr Chase puts on a show in Madrid as Bengals beat Falcons 34-17

By Matt

Ja'Marr Chase caught 10 passes for 155 yards — 142 of them in the first half — as the Bengals beat the Falcons 34-17 in Madrid, their fourth straight win and first ever outside the United States.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Madrid got the full Ja'Marr Chase experience on Sunday. The Bengals wide receiver caught 10 passes for 155 yards and a touchdown — 142 of those yards before halftime — as Cincinnati beat the Atlanta Falcons 34-17 at Real Madrid's Santiago Bernabéu Stadium in Week 9.

The win was the Bengals' fourth straight, pushed them to 6-2, and pulled them into a tie with the Pittsburgh Steelers atop the AFC North. It was also the first win outside the United States in franchise history.

Chase took over before halftime

Atlanta scored first. Michael Penix Jr. found Charlie Woerner for a one-yard touchdown on the Falcons' opening drive, the first touchdown ever scored in an NFL game in Madrid, and the Falcons led 7-0.

Cincinnati answered in five plays. Joe Burrow hit Chase on a 47-yard touchdown strike to tie it, and the Bengals never trailed again.

Chase's first half was the story of the game:

Receptions: 8; 10

Receiving yards: 142; 155

Touchdowns: 1; 1

The 142 first-half yards were the most of Chase's career in a half, and the most by a Bengal in a first half since 1988. They were also more than any player had managed against Atlanta in a full game all season. He finished with 10 catches on 12 targets.

Burrow kept dealing

Joe Burrow completed 24 of 29 passes for 271 yards and three touchdowns, and ran in a fourth score himself. It was his fourth straight game with at least three touchdown passes, and his fifth in the last six games.

Tee Higgins caught five passes for 32 yards and scored twice, both on Burrow throws. Evan McPherson added a 57-yard field goal just before halftime and a 43-yarder late to close out the scoring.

The Bengals led 24-10 at halftime and scored on all four of their first-half possessions. They have now scored 30 points or more in four straight games, something the franchise hasn't done since 1997.

The Moss company

Chase's touchdown in Madrid put him alongside Randy Moss as the only receivers in NFL history with seven or more touchdowns in each of their first six seasons.

That's the kind of company that says everything about a career, not just a game. Moss did it across his first six years in Minnesota. Chase is doing it in an era where the Bengals' offense runs through him and Burrow — and on Sunday, in front of a Madrid crowd that had never seen an NFL game before, he made it look easy.

Atlanta's side

The Falcons fell to 3-6. Bijan Robinson ran for 147 yards, and Atlanta ran for 157 as a team, but the Falcons couldn't keep pace once Cincinnati's offense got rolling. Two pass interference flags on cornerback DJ Turner II set up both Atlanta touchdowns — the only real blemish on a Bengals defensive day that ended with Barrett Carter's diving interception sealing the win.

Atlanta had scored on its first two drives. After that, Cincinnati's offense simply outscored them, and the Falcons never got closer than seven points again.

What it means

The Bengals host the Steelers next Sunday night at Paycor Stadium, with first place in the AFC North on the line. Cincinnati is playing its best football of the season, and the offense that put up 34 points in Madrid has now done it four weeks running.

The Madrid game was billed as a showcase. Chase turned it into a statement.

TSL Bets · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:04 AM ET

TSL Bets: SNF slip — Williams under 250.5, Loveland TD, Swift under 65.5 (about +790)

By Matt

The Sunday night slip is a game-script bet: Caleb Williams under 250.5 passing yards (-125) against the league's ninth-ranked pass defense, Colston Loveland to score (+165), and D'Andre Swift under 65.5 rushing yards (-115). Three legs, about +790.

The game

Tampa Bay Buccaneers (3-5) at Chicago Bears (6-2) — 8:20 PM ET, Soldier Field, NBC

Bears -6, total 47.5, moneyline Bears -270 / Buccaneers +220 (optimal-bet, DraftKings). The Bears have won three straight since Caleb Williams returned from injury — the last two against last season's Super Bowl teams, including a 34-17 win over the defending champions in Seattle on Monday night. Chicago is 6-2 against the spread and averages 387.3 total yards per game. Tampa Bay averages 300.0 total yards per game and relies on the ground game, with 168.6 rushing yards per contest against just 147.5 passing yards.

Injuries: Tampa Bay's Emeka Egbuka is good to go after practicing all week, and Bucky Irving is healthy — he ran for 170 yards last week against Atlanta. Baker Mayfield is having a miserable year by his standards: a career-worst 5.9 adjusted yards per attempt and just three touchdown passes across five games. On the Chicago side, Williams, Swift, and Loveland are all active with no injury designation reported this week — Williams missed three games earlier this season with a hamstring injury but has been hot since returning.

Storylines: Williams is 70.9% completions with six touchdowns and one interception, averaging 253.4 yards per game and 8.5 adjusted yards per attempt — legitimate MVP buzz when he is on the field. But Tampa Bay's pass defense ranks ninth in the league at 205.9 yards allowed per game, and the Bucs lead the league in time of possession with a run-heavy approach (53.5% handoffs over the last four games). The Bears are 6-point favorites at home on Sunday night, and the market expects Chicago to win — the question is how.

The legs

Leg 1 — Caleb Williams under 250.5 passing yards (-125)

Shop for the 250.5 — the consensus line is 245.5 at DraftKings and FanDuel, 246.5 at bet365, and 248.5 at BetMGM, but 250.5 is available at some books. Williams is hot: 305, 308, and 247 yards in his last three games, and he has thrown for 247 or more in each of his last six night games against NFC opponents. That is the honest caveat, and it is a real one. But the game script points under: Tampa Bay's pass defense is ninth in the league, the Bucs chew clock with the run and lead the league in time of possession, and the Bears are 6-point favorites who can win this game on the ground. OPTIMAL projects Williams at 245.7 yards. This is a game-script bet, not a talent bet — Williams is great, and the conditions still point under.

Leg 2 — Colston Loveland anytime touchdown (+165, BetMGM)

Loveland at plus money is the value leg. He is Williams' red-zone security blanket, and the Bucs' defense has been vulnerable to tight ends in the red zone all season. At +165, this is the leg that turns a solid slip into a +790 slip. If the Bears score through the air, Loveland is the most likely name on the scoresheet after the receivers — and the price says the market has not caught up to his role yet.

Leg 3 — D'Andre Swift under 65.5 rushing yards (-115, BetMGM)

Swift has 545 rushing yards and 10 rushing touchdowns in eight games, but the volume and efficiency tell a different story than the touchdown total. His game log: 124, 45, 84, 58, 43, 44, 78, 69 — under 65.5 in five of eight games. He is averaging 3.8 yards per carry, and last week against Seattle he needed 27 carries to get 69 yards. The Bears are favorites who will run the ball, but Swift's share of that work is not as big as the rushing total suggests, and the Bucs' run defense has been stout enough to hold him under this number.

Why this slip

Three legs, one game, one story: the Bears win a game that stays under the number. Williams throws for fewer than 251 yards because Tampa Bay controls the clock and the Bears lean on the run. Swift stays under 65.5 because his efficiency is poor and the workload is shared. Loveland scores because the Bears still need to finish drives, and he is the red-zone target. The legs fit together — if the game goes the way the slip needs it to, all three hit.

The honest caveat

Williams is the risk. He has thrown for 247 or more in six straight night games against NFC opponents, he is coming off 305 and 308, and he is playing at an MVP level. If he gets hot early, the under is dead by halftime. Loveland at +165 is plus money for a reason — tight end touchdowns are the most volatile prop on the board. And Swift has 10 rushing touchdowns in eight games; if he finds the end zone early, the Bears may feed him the ball. The slip pays about +790 because the Williams under is a bet against a quarterback who is playing the best football of his life.

The pick

3-leg SNF slip, about +790: Caleb Williams under 250.5 passing yards (-125) + Colston Loveland anytime TD (+165) + D'Andre Swift under 65.5 rushing yards (-115). A $10 ticket returns about $89.20. A game-script bet on Sunday night: the Bears win, the clock runs, and the numbers stay under. That is the bet.

If you or someone you know has a gambling problem, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

TSL Bets · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:04 AM ET

TSL Bets parlay: Higgins over 73.5, Lions -6.5, Broncos -2.5 (about +560)

By Matt

Three legs, about +560: Tee Higgins over 73.5 receiving yards in Madrid (-122), the Lions -6.5 in Miami (-110), and the Broncos -2.5 in Carolina (-110).

The games

Cincinnati Bengals (5-2) at Atlanta Falcons (3-5) — 9:30 AM ET, Santiago Bernabéu, Madrid, NFL Network

Bengals -3.5, total 52.5, moneyline Bengals -163 / Falcons +160 (optimal-bet). The highest total on the board this week. Injuries: Atlanta is without Brian Robinson Jr. (hamstring) and Zachariah Branch (hamstring). Cincinnati is without safety Kyle Dugger (quad). Storylines: the Bengals gave up 36 points to a Titans team that had been averaging 14.5 a game, and the Falcons lost 16-0 to the Buccaneers in a game played through heavy rain. Both teams are coming off ugly weeks, and the total of 52.5 says the market expects points in Madrid.

Detroit Lions (5-2) at Miami Dolphins (1-6) — 1:00 PM ET, Hard Rock Stadium

Lions -6.5 (-110), total 49.5, moneyline Lions -310 / Dolphins +250 (bet365). Detroit scores the third-most points in the league at 29.0 per game and ranks eighth in offensive DVOA. Miami allows the ninth-most points at 25.9 per game. Injuries: Detroit is without Jack Campbell (neck, IR), Cade Mays (ankle), D.J. Reed (hip), Kerby Joseph (knee), and D.J. Wonnum (concussion). Amon-Ra St. Brown (ribs) is questionable but is good to go. Miami's questionable list is mostly depth: Kaytron Allen (quad), Jaylen Wright (quad), and Malik Herring (neck). Storylines: the Lions' offense against one of the league's worst defenses, and a Dolphins team that has covered three of its last four — but covering and winning are different things.

Denver Broncos (6-2) at Carolina Panthers (3-4) — 1:00 PM ET, Bank of America Stadium, CBS

Broncos -2.5 (-120), total 44.5, moneyline Broncos -146 / Panthers +124 (FanDuel via Sportsbook Wire). Denver is 6-2 against the spread this season. Injuries: Denver is without Kris Abrams-Draine (ankle), Brandon Jones (hamstring), and Eyioma Uwazurike (shoulder), with Talanoa Hufanga (ankle) questionable. Carolina's offensive line is the story — Taylor Moton is on IR, Ikem Ekwonu is on PUP, Damien Lewis (knee), Jaelan Phillips (hamstring), Bobby Okereke (foot), and Will Lee III (hamstring) are all out. Bryce Young is on the injury report with an ankle issue but was a full participant. Storylines: Carolina's offense managed just 266 and 258 total yards in its last two losses, Young was sacked six times at Green Bay, and Denver's pass rush (2.9 sacks per game) faces a line that allows 3.3 sacks per game, the second-most in the league. The Broncos have won and covered both of their road games this season.

The legs

Leg 1 — Tee Higgins over 73.5 receiving yards (-122, BetRivers)

Higgins has 43 catches for 726 yards and four touchdowns in seven games. He had 10 catches for 155 yards last week against Tennessee, and 303 yards in the two games since the bye. He has topped 90 yards in all but two games this season. The Falcons rank 24th in DVOA against No. 2 wide receivers and allow 148.2 yards per game to the position. This is the highest total on the board, in a dome-like atmosphere in Madrid, against a defense that gives up yards to receivers. Higgins over 73.5 is the cleanest receiving prop on the slate.

Leg 2 — Detroit Lions -6.5 (-110)

The Lions score 29 points a game. The Dolphins give up 25.9. Detroit is the better team by a touchdown on paper, and the spread is 6.5. Miami has covered three of its last four, but the Lions' offense is the kind that turns a cover into a blowout — and the Dolphins' injury list is mostly on the defensive side of the ball. This is the chalk leg, and it is chalk for a reason.

Leg 3 — Denver Broncos -2.5 (-110)

The Broncos are 6-2 against the spread, they have won and covered both road games, and they are playing a Panthers team whose offensive line is held together with tape and whose quarterback was sacked six times last week. Denver's pass rush against Carolina's line is the biggest mismatch in this game, and a short road number of 2.5 does not reflect how far apart these teams are right now. Covers' top pick is the Broncos.

Why these three together

Three different games, three different kinds of bet, one story: the better team, the better receiver, and the better matchup, all at fair prices. The parlay pays about +560 because each leg is a real bet on its own, and putting them together is how you get paid for being right three times.

The honest caveat

Parlays multiply risk, not just payout. Higgins has to clear 73.5 against a Falcons defense that, for all its flaws, just shut out Tampa Bay — even if that was in the rain. The Lions are laying 6.5 on the road against a Dolphins team that has covered three of four, and Detroit's defense is missing four starters. The Broncos are laying points on the road with a banged-up secondary of their own. Any one of these legs can lose on its own. That is why the price is +560 and not -560. Bet it as a fun ticket on three good reads, not as a mortgage payment.

The pick

3-leg parlay, about +560: Tee Higgins over 73.5 receiving yards (-122) + Detroit Lions -6.5 (-110) + Denver Broncos -2.5 (-110). A $10 ticket returns about $66.30. A morning prop in Madrid, a road favorite in Miami, and a road favorite in Carolina. That is the bet.

TSL Bets: 4pm window slip — JSN TD, Bowers TD, Texans -5.5 (about +725)

By Matt

The 4pm window slip is the stars-and-chalk slip: Jaxon Smith-Njigba to score (-125) against the league's leakiest pass defense, Brock Bowers to score (+140) against a banged-up 49ers secondary, and the Texans -5.5 (-110) against a winless Chargers team. Three legs, about +725.

The games

Arizona Cardinals (3-5) at Seattle Seahawks (4-4) — 4:25 PM ET, Lumen Field, FOX

Seahawks -8.5, total 39.5, moneyline Seahawks -420 / Cardinals +340 (Win Daily, FanDuel/DraftKings). Seattle opened as a 13.5-point favorite; the line dropped to 8.5 when Sam Darnold was ruled out, and the total fell from 44.5 to 39.5. Darnold is out with a quad injury and a cracked rib suffered Monday night against Chicago, so Drew Lock starts — and Lock has already beaten Arizona this season, 31-7 in Week 2, with three touchdowns and a 139.1 passer rating. Gardner Minshew starts for the Cardinals. Injuries: Arizona is without Bam Knight (concussion), James Conner (foot, IR), Trey Benson (knee, IR), Paris Johnson Jr., Will Johnson, and Kendrick Bourne. Seattle is without Darnold, Charles Cross (ankle), Quenton Nelson (knee), Leonard Williams (stinger), Uchenna Nwosu (ankle), and Rashid Shaheed (knee). Storylines: Jaxon Smith-Njigba leads the NFL in receiving yards and receiving touchdowns; the Cardinals have allowed 18 passing touchdowns (tied for the most in the league) and 29 total touchdowns through eight games (the most); and Lock is 72.9% completions with four touchdowns and zero interceptions this season.

Las Vegas Raiders (4-4) at San Francisco 49ers (6-1) — 4:25 PM ET, Levi's Stadium, FOX

49ers -9.5, total 52.5, moneyline 49ers -475 / Raiders +364 (optimal-bet). The Raiders score 27.6 points per game, and Kirk Cousins ranks top-10 in EPA per play and success rate. Injuries: Las Vegas has a clean report — Thomas Booker (ankle) is out, but all offensive starters are good to go. San Francisco is banged up: Mike Evans (Achilles), Christian Kirk (knee), and Deebo Samuel (hamstring) are all out, along with Ricky Pearsall, and Upton Stout (shoulder) and Nate Hobbs (adductor) are questionable. Storylines: the 49ers are 9.5-point favorites with their top three receivers out; the Raiders' offense is the healthiest unit on the field; and the total of 52.5 says the market expects points even with San Francisco's injuries.

Houston Texans (2-5) at Los Angeles Chargers (0-7) — 4:05 PM ET, SoFi Stadium, CBS

Texans -5.5 (-110), total 40.5, moneyline Texans -235 / Chargers +200 (Win Daily, FanDuel/BetMGM). The Chargers opened as 2.5-point favorites; the line has reversed all the way to Houston -6 at some books. Injuries: Houston is without David Montgomery (groin) and Reed Blankenship (concussion/hand). Los Angeles is without Derwin James Jr. (hamstring), Rashawn Slater (ankle), Deane Leonard (concussion), and Kendall Williamson (calf), with Brenen Thompson (quad/hamstring) and David Njoku (fibula) doubtful. Storylines: the Chargers are 0-7, score a league-worst 14.1 points per game, and Justin Herbert has five touchdowns against 10 interceptions while being sacked 23 times. C.J. Stroud has 11 touchdowns against one interception for 1,867 yards. Covers' top pick is Texans -5.5.

The legs

Leg 1 — Jaxon Smith-Njigba anytime touchdown (-125, DraftKings)

JSN leads the NFL with 985 receiving yards, 64 catches, and nine receiving touchdowns — the most in the league. He has scored in six of eight games, averages 123.1 yards per game, and has topped 102 yards in seven of eight. Against Arizona specifically, with Lock at quarterback, he went 8-for-122-and-1 and 9-for-155-and-3 in Week 2. The Cardinals have allowed the most touchdowns in the NFL. This is the best receiver in football against the worst pass defense in football, at -125.

Leg 2 — Brock Bowers anytime touchdown (+140, Sports Interaction)

Bowers at plus money is the value leg of the slip. The Raiders score 27.6 points per game, Cousins is playing efficient football, and the 49ers' secondary is held together with tape — their top three receivers are out, but so is the depth behind them, and the defense has a bevy of injury question marks. Bowers is the Raiders' best red-zone weapon, and +140 for a touchdown from a tight end in a game with a 52.5 total is a fair price.

Leg 3 — Houston Texans -5.5 (-110)

The one spread anchor in the slip, and it is the most lopsided matchup on the board. The Chargers are 0-7, cannot score, cannot protect their quarterback, and are missing Derwin James and Rashawn Slater. The Texans are 2-5 but a much better team than their record — Stroud is playing clean football, and the line has moved from Chargers -2.5 to Texans -6, which is the market telling you everything. Laying 5.5 on the road is never fun, but this is the right spot for it.

Why this slip

Two star-player touchdown legs at fair prices, plus a spread anchor on the most one-sided game of the window. JSN and Bowers are both the best red-zone options on their teams, and both are playing defenses that give up touchdowns. The Texans leg is the chalk that holds the slip together — it does not need to be pretty, it needs to be a win by a touchdown against a team that has not won a game all season.

The honest caveat

Drew Lock is the risk in leg one. He has been good this season, and he torched Arizona in Week 2, but he is a backup quarterback with an elbow injury who was limited in practice all week. If Lock struggles, JSN's volume shrinks. Bowers at +140 is plus money for a reason — tight end touchdowns are volatile, and the 49ers' defense is still good even when it is hurt. And the Texans are laying 5.5 on the road, which is always a live number against a desperate team. The slip pays about +725 because all three legs have to hit, and the Bowers leg is a coin flip at best.

The pick

3-leg 4pm window slip, about +725: Jaxon Smith-Njigba anytime TD (-125) + Brock Bowers anytime TD (+140) + Houston Texans -5.5 (-110). A $10 ticket returns about $82.50. The best receiver in football, the best tight end in football, and the worst team in football. That is the bet.

TSL Bets: 1pm window slip — Kyren TD, Puka over 92.5, Cowboys-Colts over 51.5 (about +500)

By Matt

The 1pm window slip leans on the Rams' run game in Washington and a shootout in Indianapolis: Kyren Williams to score (-145), Puka Nacua over 92.5 receiving yards (-114), and Cowboys-Colts over 51.5 (-110). Three legs, about +500.

The games

Los Angeles Rams (4-4) at Washington Commanders (2-4-1) — 1:00 PM ET, Northwest Stadium, FOX

Rams -4.5 (-110), total 47.5, moneyline Rams -235 / Commanders +191 (Action Network). The Rams have won two straight, 24-9 over the Chargers last week, and are 4-4 against the spread. Washington is coming off a convincing win over Philadelphia and is 4-3 ATS. The injury flag: Matthew Stafford was icing his shoulder after the Chargers win, and there is a chance he is not at 100%. That matters for the passing game — it does not matter much for the leg we are playing. On the Washington side, the defensive front is injury-depleted, which is exactly why the Rams' run game is the play. Storylines: the Rams' offense has found its identity on the ground, with Kyren Williams and Blake Corum becoming the keys to the attack; Washington's defense ranks as the second-leakiest in the league in passes to running backs relative to expectation; and the spread is close enough to keep Stafford honest, which keeps the Rams in scoring position all afternoon.

Dallas Cowboys (3-5) at Indianapolis Colts (2-6) — 1:00 PM ET, Lucas Oil Stadium, FOX

Cowboys -4.5, total 51.5, moneyline Cowboys -212 / Colts +208 (optimal-bet). Dallas is 5-2 to the over this season; Indianapolis is 4-3 to the over. The Colts have lost four straight, including a 42-7 loss to Jacksonville last week. Injuries: Dallas is without KaVontae Turpin (ribs) and Malachi Lawrence (ankle), and George Pickens (hamstring), Jake Ferguson (ankle), DeMarvion Overshown (hamstring/back), and Joey Porter Jr. (ankle) are all questionable. Indianapolis is without Akeem Davis-Gaither (knee) and Justin Walley (knee), with Jalen Travis (thumb) questionable and Cam Taylor-Britt (groin) on injured reserve. Storylines: both defenses are rotten — Dallas plays at the fastest pace in the league, the Colts have allowed 30 or more points in four games, and Indianapolis ranks 27th in defensive DVOA. The total has held at 51.5 all week, and the market expects points.

The legs

Leg 1 — Kyren Williams anytime touchdown (-145, Fanatics)

Williams has 112 carries for 548 yards and six touchdowns, averaging 4.9 yards per carry, and he has found the end zone in consecutive games. He is the most likely scorer in this game at Fanatics, and the matchup is right: Washington's defensive front is depleted, the Rams are 4.5-point favorites, and Williams is the goal-line back in an offense that is leaning on the run. The Stafford shoulder flag does not touch this leg — if anything, a limited Stafford means more Williams near the goal line.

Leg 2 — Puka Nacua over 92.5 receiving yards (-114, FanDuel)

Nacua is healthy with no injury designation, and he is the Rams' primary target with Davante Adams on injured reserve — 19 catches and two touchdowns in his last two games. The projection is roughly 9-10 receptions for about 96 yards. Washington's secondary has been leaky all season, and Nacua is the kind of receiver who gets there on volume alone. Even if Stafford is not at 100%, Nacua is the first read on nearly every dropback.

Leg 3 — Cowboys at Colts over 51.5 (-110)

Two bad defenses, one fast-paced offense, and a total that has not moved off 51.5. Dallas is 5-2 to the over this season. The Colts have allowed 30-plus in four games. Both teams can score — the question is whether either can stop the other, and the answer this season has been no. This is the shootout leg of the slip, and it is the one with the most margin for error: 52 points between two teams that both play fast and neither of which tackles well.

Why this slip

Two legs ride the Rams' offense in a game they should control, and the third rides a total that two bad defenses should clear. The legs are correlated in a good way — if the Rams score, Williams and Nacua are the two most likely reasons — but they are not the same bet three times. Williams needs one trip to the end zone. Nacua needs a big receiving day. The Cowboys and Colts need to do what they have done all season: score, and let the other team score.

The honest caveat

The Stafford shoulder is the real risk in this slip. If he is limited, the Rams' passing game shrinks, and Nacua's over gets harder — though Williams' leg gets easier. The Cowboys-Colts over is the other risk: 51.5 is a big number, and if either team has an off day, or if the questionable tags on Pickens and Ferguson turn into absences, the scoring dries up. And Williams at -145 is a short price for a reason — he has to actually get in. Nothing here is a lock. The slip pays about +500 because all three legs have to hit.

The pick

3-leg 1pm window slip, about +500: Kyren Williams anytime TD (-145) + Puka Nacua over 92.5 receiving yards (-114) + Cowboys-Colts over 51.5 (-110). A $10 ticket returns about $60.60. The Rams' run game, the Rams' No. 1 receiver, and two defenses that cannot stop anyone. That is the bet.

Gaming · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

World of Warcraft: Forever is the biggest launch in WoW history

By Matt

World of Warcraft: Forever launched November 4, and Blizzard says it is the biggest launch in the game's history — by peak concurrent players and returning players — with the most new players for a WoW launch in 15 years.

What happened

World of Warcraft: Forever is the biggest launch in World of Warcraft history.

Blizzard announced on Saturday, November 7, that the new version of WoW — which launched globally on Tuesday, November 4 at 3:00 p.m. PST, all regions at once — set records for peak concurrent players and for the highest number of returning players in the game's history. The company did not release exact figures, but the scale of the claim is the point: in a game that has been running for 22 years, this is the biggest launch it has ever had.

Executive producer Holly Longdale added another detail: Forever also drew the most new players for a WoW launch in 15 years. And in China, NetEase reported more than one million concurrent players — a record for the game in that region.

What Forever actually is

Forever is the third ongoing version of World of Warcraft, sitting alongside Retail and Classic. It is included in the existing subscription — no separate purchase — and it takes players back to the original 2004 version of Azeroth, with a level cap of 60, plus new zones, quests, dungeons, and raids built on top of that foundation.

The idea is the one Blizzard has been circling for years: the vanilla world, rebuilt with modern systems and new content, for players who want the old map without the old grind. The beta was the largest in franchise history, and the launch itself went smoothly — short queues, no major outages, which for a WoW launch is its own kind of news.

Why the numbers matter

WoW launches are usually measured in two ways: how many people show up, and how many of them stay. Blizzard is only claiming the first one so far — peak concurrents, returning players, new players — and it is not sharing the raw numbers. But the combination is notable: a record for returning players and the most new players in 15 years means Forever is pulling in both directions at once, luring back lapsed players and bringing in people who have never played.

The China number is the most concrete data point. One million concurrent players in a single region, on a single day, is a scale that very few games ever reach. NetEase's record suggests the launch was not just a Western nostalgia event.

What it means

World of Warcraft has been declared dead more times than any game in history, and it keeps answering with launches like this one. Forever is a bet that the original Azeroth — the map, the pace, the world — still has pull, and the first numbers say the bet is paying off. The real test is what happens in a month, when the launch rush fades and the question becomes whether players are still logging in. For now, though, Blizzard can say something it has never said before: this is the biggest launch the game has ever had.

The details

Launch: November 4, 2026, 3:00 p.m. PST, global

Record: Biggest launch in WoW history (peak concurrent players, returning players)

New players: Most for a WoW launch in 15 years (per Holly Longdale)

China: 1M+ concurrent players, a record (per NetEase)

Format: Third ongoing version, alongside Retail and Classic; included in subscription

Level cap: 60, original 2004 Azeroth + new zones, quests, dungeons, raids

Collectibles · Nov 8, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

Pokémon printed 10 billion cards a year — and still sold out

By Matt

The Pokémon Company printed roughly 10 billion cards in the fiscal year ending March 2026 — past 85 billion lifetime — and it still wasn't enough. Output has been flat for three years because the printers are already at maximum capacity, and the next big plant doesn't open until 2028.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Pokémon Company printed roughly 10 billion Pokémon TCG cards in the fiscal year ending March 31, 2026. The lifetime total is now past 85 billion cards since the game launched in 1996. The cards are printed in 16 languages and sold in more than 90 countries.

And it still was not enough. Shops are still selling out on day one.

That is the number at the center of the story: 10 billion sounds like a flood, but it is a ceiling. Output has been flat at around 10 billion a year for three years — the high-water mark was about 11.9 billion in fiscal 2023/24 — because the company has been printing at maximum capacity since January 2025. There is no more room on the presses. The only way to make more cards is to build more presses, and that takes years.

The sellout problem

The demand side is not a mystery. A London shop's allocation rose 40% and still sold out on day one, according to a Brit Brief report from a London Comic Con panel on November 6. That is the pattern everywhere: allocations go up, demand goes up faster, and the shelves are empty by the afternoon.

The timing makes it worse. Mega Evolution – Delta Reign and the 30th Celebration set launched on November 6, right into the holiday season, when demand is at its peak. New sets used to be a restock event. Now they are a race, and the race is over before most people get to the store.

The fix is coming — slowly

The Pokémon Company is building a new printing campus: 1.27 million square feet, due for completion in 2027, with full operation expected in late 2028. When it is running, it would roughly double the company's printing capacity.

That is a real fix, and it is also two years away. Between now and then, the math does not change: 10 billion cards a year, flat, against demand that keeps growing. The company is not holding back supply to create hype — it is printing as fast as it physically can, and it is still not enough.

What it means

For collectors, the takeaway is simple: the scarcity is structural, not artificial. The cards are not hard to find because the company is being cute with supply. They are hard to find because the printers are maxed out and the next plant is two years from full operation. That means day-one sellouts are the normal state of things until 2028, and the secondary market is where the action is — for better and for worse.

Ten billion cards a year. Eighty-five billion lifetime. And still sold out. That is the Pokémon economy right now.

Shop the drop

Looking for the latest Pokémon TCG releases? Pokémon TCG on Amazon

The numbers

Printed FY2025/26: ~10 billion cards

Lifetime total: Past 85 billion (since 1996)

Output trend: Flat ~10B/year for 3 years (high ~11.9B in FY23/24)

Capacity: Maximum since January 2025

Languages / countries: 16 languages, 90+ countries

New plant: 1.27M sq ft campus, completion 2027, full operation late 2028

2-Leg Parlay (about +257): Spurs -5.5 + Canadiens Moneyline

By Matt

Two plays, one story: the league's hottest big man against a shorthanded backcourt, and the East's hottest team at a coin-flip price. Together they pay about +257.

The legs

Leg 1 — San Antonio Spurs -5.5 (-110)

San Antonio Spurs at Portland Trail Blazers, 10:00 PM ET. Portland is down Ja Morant, Shaedon Sharpe, and Vit Krejci on the second night of a back-to-back, and Victor Wembanyama has scored 82 points in his last two games — 52 against Dallas on Wednesday, 30 against Sacramento on Friday. Leg 2 — Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115)

Montreal Canadiens at Toronto Maple Leafs, 7:00 PM ET. The Canadiens are 10-3-3, have won six of seven and four straight over Toronto, and start Jacob Fowler (7-1-1, .925 save percentage). The Leafs have lost three of four and are held below 30 shots a night lately. Tavares is expected back for Toronto, but one player returning from three weeks out doesn't close the gap at a coin-flip price.

Why these two together

Neither asks for a miracle. Put them together and the price jumps to about +257 — a $10 ticket returns about $35.70 — which is the whole point: two solid reads, one ticket.

The Canadiens are the better team, but the Leafs are rested, at home, on a Saturday night, with their captain back. Either leg can lose on its own. That is why the price is +257 and not -257. Bet it as a fun ticket on two good reads, not as a mortgage payment.

The pick

2-leg parlay, about +257: San Antonio Spurs -5.5 (-110) + Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115). The hottest big man in the league and the hottest team in the East, both at fair prices. That is the bet.

Canadiens Moneyline (-115): The East's hottest team against a slumping rival — even with Tavares back

By Matt

Montreal is 10-3-3, has won six of seven and four straight over Toronto, and starts a goalie at 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage. The Leafs get Tavares back, but -115 is still the value side.

Game slip

Montreal Canadiens (10-3-3) at Toronto Maple Leafs (8-7-2)

Saturday, November 7, 2026 — 7:00 PM ET — Scotiabank Arena, Toronto, ON — ESPN+, SN, TVAS

Total: 6.5

Moneyline: Canadiens -115 / Maple Leafs +105

Odds source: ESPN via DraftKings, Nov. 7

### Injury report

Montreal: RW Ivan Demidov (ankle) — injured reserve. D Mike Matheson (lower body) — out. G Samuel Montembeault (lower body) — injured reserve, day-to-day, could return after a month out. D Arber Xhekaj (undisclosed) — questionable. Expected starter Jacob Fowler is 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage and a 2.17 goals-against average.

Toronto: C John Tavares (lower body) — expected to return after missing nine games since October 15. He practiced Friday in a regular jersey, skated on a line with Nick Paul and William Nylander, and rejoined the top power-play unit. RW Kirill Marchenko (lower body) — injured reserve. C Max Domi (back) — injured reserve. F Miles Wood — injured reserve. D Nick Blankenburg — injured reserve. G Elvis Merzlikins (shoulder) — injured reserve.

### Storylines

• Montreal is the hottest team in the East right now: 10-3-3, winners of six of seven, coming off a 5-4 overtime win over Utah on Thursday in which Nick Suzuki returned from a six-game absence with three points and Cole Caufield scored the winner 30 seconds into OT.

• The Canadiens carry a four-game win streak over the Maple Leafs into Scotiabank Arena, and they just showed they can win the hard way — rallying from 2-0 and 4-3 down on Thursday, two nights after blowing a 4-0 lead to Winnipeg.

• Toronto is slumping: 8-7-2, losers of three of four, held below 30 shots in four straight games, and 4-4-1 at home. The middle six has buckled with Tavares and Marchenko out, and the Leafs are starting a stretch of five straight games against teams in a playoff position.

• The goalie matchup tilts Montreal's way. Fowler is 7-1-1 with a .925 save percentage; one starting-goalie service projects Sergei Bobrovsky for Toronto at .889 with a 3.27 goals-against average. That is a big gap in a game lined as a coin flip.

The bet

Montreal Canadiens moneyline (-115)

This is a bet on the better team at a coin-flip price. Montreal is better at even strength, better in goal, deeper down the lineup, and has owned this matchup lately — four straight wins over Toronto. The Leafs' best argument is Tavares returning and three days of rest, and both are real. But Tavares is one player coming back from a three-week absence, likely on the wing for the first time, and rest doesn't fix a team that has been outshot and outworked for a week. At -115, you're getting the 10-3-3 team at the price of a toss-up. Take it.

Why

The market is pricing the name on the front of the jersey, not the team on the ice. Toronto at home, on Hockey Night in Canada, against Montreal — that matchup always draws Leafs money, and it has kept this line near even despite everything in the form guide pointing the other way. Montreal has the better record by a distance, the better goalie by a distance, the head-to-head streak, and the momentum of a team that just stared down a brutal loss and answered with a comeback win. The Habs' blue line is banged up, but Fowler has covered for it all season.

The honest caveat

Tavares matters. He had seven points in seven games before the injury, he is a point-per-game player for his career, and his return immediately fixes Toronto's top power play and its faceoff circle. The Leafs have also had three days off since their 3-2 loss to Utah on November 3, while Montreal played Thursday and had one day of rest. And Montreal is missing Demidov, Matheson, and possibly Montembeault — if Fowler has an off night, there is no safety net. A rested Leafs team with its captain back, at home, on a Saturday night, is a live underdog. The price is fair, not a steal.

The pick

Canadiens moneyline (-115). The better team, the better goalie, the better form — at a coin-flip price.

Spurs -5.5 (-110): A shorthanded Blazers backcourt meets the hottest big man alive

By Matt

Portland is down Morant, Sharpe, and Krejci on the second night of a back-to-back, and Victor Wembanyama just put up 52 and 30 in his last two games. Laying 5.5 with the Spurs is the side.

Game slip

San Antonio Spurs (7-2) at Portland Trail Blazers (6-2)

Saturday, November 7, 2026 — 10:00 PM ET — Moda Center, Portland, OR — DAZN-SAS, KUNP, BlazerVision

Spread: Spurs -5.5

Total: 227.5

Moneyline: Spurs -202 / Trail Blazers +170

Odds source: Consensus across major US books, Nov. 7

### Injury report

San Antonio: F Tobias Harris (calf) — out. F/C Jayden Quaintance (knee) — out. Neither has played this season, so the rotation is effectively intact: De'Aaron Fox, Stephon Castle, Dylan Harper, Devin Vassell, and Victor Wembanyama are all averaging 30-plus minutes. Wembanyama rolled his ankle near the end of the first half Friday against Sacramento but finished the game — worth watching, not a reason to fade.

Portland: G Ja Morant (Grade 2 left hamstring strain) — out. G/F Shaedon Sharpe (torn meniscus) — out. F Vit Krejci (left leg fracture) — out. G/F John Tonje — out. That leaves Damian Lillard, Scoot Henderson, and Jrue Holiday to cover the guard minutes, and Henderson is shooting 31.7% from the field this season.

### Storylines

• Wembanyama is on a tear that barely needs adjectives. He dropped 52 points, 24 rebounds, and 8 blocks on Dallas on November 5, then followed it with 30 points and 14 rebounds in Friday's 124-109 win over Sacramento. He is averaging 29.1 points per game this season and 34.6 over his last five.

• Both teams are on the second night of a back-to-back. The difference is who is missing: Portland just lost 124-118 to the Lakers on Friday night in Los Angeles, with Lillard and Deni Avdija each playing heavy minutes, and now comes home to face the West's hottest team without three rotation players.

• The Spurs are 7-2 and playing like the team that won the Western Conference last spring. Fox, Castle, Harper, and Vassell give San Antonio four more creators around Wembanyama; Portland's shorthanded backcourt has one.

• Portland is 6-2 for a reason — this team competes — and the Moda Center is a real home-court edge. The Blazers beat the Lakers in Los Angeles on October 27 and took them to the wire again Friday. They will not roll over.

The bet

San Antonio Spurs -5.5 (-110)

The number is fair, and the matchup is not. Portland's three best answers for Wembanyama's gravity — Morant's pace control, Sharpe's athleticism, Krejci's size on the wing — are all in street clothes. The Blazers' remaining guards shot a combined 31.7% (Henderson) on a night when they needed everything to go right just to hang with the Lakers. Now they get Wembanyama, who has scored 82 points in his last two games, on no rest, with a frontcourt rotation that has no answer for him. The Spurs' depth wins the minutes when Wembanyama sits, and Wembanyama wins the minutes when he plays.

Why

The math is simple and it is on our side. San Antonio is the better team at full strength, and Portland is nowhere near full strength. The Spurs' floor is high — four guards who can create, a center who bends the entire defense, and a defense that travels. Portland's 6-2 record is real, but it was built with Morant and Sharpe in the lineup. Without them, the Blazers are a different team, and the market knows it: this line opened at -4.5 and has been bet up to -5.5.

The honest caveat

Laying 5.5 on the road, on the second night of a back-to-back, is never free money. Wembanyama rolled his ankle Friday — he finished the game and scored 30, but ankles are ankles, and if he is limited or sits, this ticket is in trouble. Portland is 6-2, tough at home, and just played the Lakers to a six-point game with this same shorthanded group. And the total (227.5) tells you the market expects points, which means a backdoor cover is always live if the Spurs ease off late. This is a bet on the better, healthier team — not a lock.

The pick

Spurs -5.5 (-110). The hottest player in the league against a backcourt held together with tape. Lay the points.

Air Jordan 6 'White Infrared' drops today — MJ's first championship shoe returns with Nike Air on the heel

By Elijah

The OG 'White Infrared' Jordan 6 returns today, November 7, for $215 — the first retro since 2014, and the first since 1991 to bring back the Nike Air branding on the heel.

The Air Jordan 6 Retro "White Infrared" drops today, Saturday, November 7, 2026 — and for Jordan collectors, this is the one circled on the calendar all month. The OG colorway from 1991 returns in full-family sizing for $215 for men ($155 grade school, $95 preschool, $80 toddler), through Nike and select Jordan retailers.

This is the shoe Michael Jordan wore to his first championship. Tinker Hatfield designed the 6 with a streamlined shape inspired by German sports cars, and the "White Infrared" — crisp white leather, sharp red accents — is one of the model's original colorways. It has been 35 years since the debut, and 12 years since the last retro in 2014 (before that, 2010). For a lot of collectors, this is the first real shot at the pair in over a decade.

What makes this retro different

The detail that matters most: Nike Air is back on the heel. The 2010 and 2014 retros swapped the original Nike Air heel branding for a Jumpman logo. This release returns to the original construction, with the Nike Air graphic embroidered in white on the heel — the way the 1991 pair looked.

The rest of the shoe is faithful to the OG too. A crisp white leather upper with the 6's signature zig-zag structure and perforated panels across the sides, Infrared details on the midsole, the lace toggle, and the Jumpman logo on the tongue. Style code CT8529-105.

Where to get them

The "White Infrared" 6 is a wide release — Nike SNKRS and select Jordan Brand retailers, in-store and online, in full-family sizing. At $215 for men, it is also one of the more accessible grails on the November calendar, and pre-release pairs were already listed under retail in some sizes in the weeks leading up to the drop.

If you only buy one Jordan in November, the release calendars are near-unanimous: this is the one.

Gaming · Nov 7, 2026 · 7:55 AM ET

Ocarina of Time remake is 2026's highest-rated game — a 96 on Metacritic

By Matt

Nintendo's ground-up Switch 2 remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time launched November 5 to a 96 Metascore — the highest-rated game of 2026, and one of the 30 highest-rated games in Metacritic history.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time is back, and the critics have spoken about as loudly as critics can speak. Nintendo's ground-up remake for Switch 2 launched Thursday, November 5, 2026, carrying a 96 on Metacritic — the highest-rated game of 2026, built from 87 critic reviews with zero mixed or negative notices among them. Players agree so far: the user score sits at 9.6.

The remake is a Switch 2 exclusive, $59.99 digital and $69.99 physical. It is, by the numbers, one of the best-reviewed games ever made — sitting inside Metacritic's all-time top 30, one rung below the original it remakes.

The original is still the one to beat

Here is the beautiful problem with remaking Ocarina of Time: the 1998 Nintendo 64 original holds a 99 on Metacritic, still the highest aggregate score the site has ever recorded for any game. The remake's 96 doesn't topple its own legend. It does, however, clear Nintendo's last attempt by a comfortable margin — Ocarina of Time 3D on the 3DS sits at 94 — and it puts the remake ahead of every other 2026 release, including Resident Evil Requiem, Phantom Blade Zero, Forza Horizon 6, and The Witcher 3 Remastered.

The review spread tells the story. The Guardian, Eurogamer, GamesRadar, and Destructoid all handed out perfect scores. IGN, GameSpot, and Forbes each gave it 9 out of 10. One outlet's verdict, quoted widely this week, called it "the gold standard for any remake of any franchise going forward."

What Nintendo actually built

This is not a remaster. Nintendo rebuilt the game from the ground up for Switch 2, with overhauled visuals across every dungeon and landmark, reworked camera and movement controls, new character dialogue, and a symphonic orchestral soundtrack. It targets and holds 60 frames per second in both docked and handheld modes, and it integrates with the Nintendo Switch App's Zelda Notes service for interactive maps and progress tracking.

There are new additions for returning players, too. A new Hall of Fallen Foes lets players view statues of the enemies they have defeated — a small touch, but the kind that tells you the developers were thinking about people who have already beaten this game three times and want a reason to do it a fourth. Nintendo's own Ask the Developer interview, published around launch, goes deep on just how much work the rebuild took.

Not everything made the cut. The lack of Master Quest — the harder alternative dungeon mode included in previous releases — is the most common complaint in otherwise glowing reviews.

Launch week had its wrinkles

No launch this big goes perfectly. Within hours of release, the remake had been dumped and run on a PC emulator, continuing a pattern that has shadowed Nintendo's biggest Switch 2 launches. And some Amazon physical pre-orders were delayed by up to a week after launch day, leaving buyers waiting while the digital version went live on schedule.

Neither wrinkle has dented the reception. At 96, the remake is also the most critically acclaimed game on Switch 2 to date — a remarkable achievement for a game that first came out before a lot of today's Switch 2 owners were even born.

adidas x BAPE's Crazy Energy+ Leads Its Y2K Hoops Drop

By Matt

BAPE and adidas drop their Y2K hoops collection today — the Crazy Energy+ in metallic silver camo leads, fronted by Jalen Williams, with purple camo apparel and a second shoe in tow.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

BAPE and adidas are back together, and this time they've swapped the football pitch for the hardwood. Their new collection — a late-'90s and early-2000s basketball-inspired range fronted by Oklahoma City Thunder star Jalen Williams — drops today, November 7, and the shoe leading it is the adidas Crazy Energy+ in metallic silver camo.

The shoe

The Crazy Energy+ is one of adidas Basketball's newest performance models, and BAPE's version covers its sculpted upper in an all-over metallic print the brand calls COLOR CAMO SILVER — a tonal abstract ape camo with ape faces and Trefoils faintly visible in the pattern. A knitted "WGM" overlay sits where the laces would normally dominate, a nod to World Gone Mad, BAPE's long-running slogan, and the pair ships with two interchangeable lace options. Underneath the camo it's still a real hoops shoe, with Lightstrike Pro cushioning and a moulded base.

The details, as listed by release trackers:

Model: BAPE x adidas Crazy Energy+

Colorway: Silver Metallic / Silver Metallic / Silver Metallic

Style code: KH8660

Release date: November 7, 2026

Price: $200 (listed by most trackers; see note below)

One honest flag on price: most release trackers list the Crazy Energy+ at $200, but at least one outlet has it at $150. Check the CONFIRMED app on drop day before you commit.

The rest of the collection

The apparel leans on COLOR CAMO PURPLE — the purple camo print fans link to BAPE's early-2000s peak — led by a reversible mesh shark hoodie. The range also includes a statement chenille varsity jacket in black with leather sleeves, adidas Originals and BAPE ape-head patches, and big chenille "A" and "B" letters across the chest.

There's a second shoe in the drop, too: the adidas Adizero Goukana (KH8096), confirmed to also release November 7 via BAPE's retail channels, in bright pink, purple, and red tones with a large white STA motif.

When and where

The full footwear and apparel range releases today, November 7, via the adidas CONFIRMED app, select retailers, BAPE STORE locations, and BAPE.COM.

Why this one matters

The interesting call here is the silhouette itself. adidas and BAPE could have reached into the archive for this one — a Forum or a Superstar in camo would have been the safe play. Instead they put BAPE's treatment on a current performance basketball shoe, one that only launched earlier this year. The base Crazy Energy debuted in July 2026 at $110 in the Color Shift and Ice White colorways, built on full-length Lightstrike cushioning, and it's been worn on court this season by members of the adidas family — including Jalen Williams, who fronts this campaign. We first got a look at the silhouette when Steph Curry debuted it on court during his sneaker free agency season last year.

The timing fits the era the collection is chasing. The late '90s and early 2000s were when basketball style carried as much weight off the court as on it — baggy mesh, loud camo, varsity jackets — and BAPE was right in the middle of it. This drop is that whole wardrobe at once: silver camo on court, purple camo off it. It's also the latest in a busy year between the two brands, following their earlier 2026 project tied to last summer's global football tournament.

Steam Drops Four Free Games to Keep

By Matt

Four very different indie games — led by the 99%-rated Heartfall — are free to download and keep on Steam starting today.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Steam's newest round of free games is live starting today, November 6, 2026 — four games, free to download and keep forever. No catch, no subscription, no time limit on claiming them. Just add them to your library and they're yours.

This is part of a steady stream of free games Steam has delivered throughout 2026, and this week's set is a strong one: a story-driven visual novel RPG, a cosmic-horror cooking game, a found-footage horror short, and a post-apocalyptic tactical RPG. Four very different experiences, and every one of them carries an overwhelmingly positive rating from Steam users.

The four free games

Heartfall: A Tale in Three Lives: 99% positive; Story-driven visual novel RPG

Cucina Stellata: 97% positive; Psychological horror cooking game

Playable Archive: 97% positive; First-person found-footage horror

Outcast Tales: 95% positive; Post-apocalyptic tactical RPG

Heartfall: A Tale in Three Lives — 99% positive

The headliner of the set, and the highest-rated game of the four. Heartfall is a story-driven visual novel RPG from developer Kazehita that follows a character named Grian after he wakes up on a mysterious island. Players explore different storylines, form alliances, uncover secrets, and make decisions that change how the adventure unfolds.

The scale is what stands out: it's a fan project inspired by the YouTube series 3rd Life, featuring over 400,000 words of dialogue, 450 illustrations, and more than 50 original music tracks. A 99% positive rating puts it among the highest-rated free games on Steam — the kind of number usually reserved for established classics, not a free fan project.

Cucina Stellata — 97% positive

The weirdest game in the set, and maybe the most interesting. Developed by Filthy Eden and published by CRITICAL REFLEX, Cucina Stellata is a psychological horror experience that combines traditional Italian cooking with cosmic horror. Players prepare authentic 19th-century Italian recipes in what the game describes as "a kitchen haunted by hunger."

It starts out looking like a straightforward cooking game, then descends into disturbing imagery and strange supernatural elements. It's currently sitting at 97% positive across more than 1,100 Steam reviews, and it's short — about an hour — which makes it an easy weekend pickup.

Playable Archive — 97% positive

For horror fans looking for something different, Playable Archive is a first-person adventure from DarkStone Digital built around mysterious footage of a New England couple who discover a strange object while renovating their home. Players investigate disturbing events — demonic rituals, communicating with the dead — across multiple timelines.

The whole game runs 30–60 minutes and has to be completed in a single sitting, which fits the found-footage format perfectly. It also holds a 97% positive rating on Steam.

Outcast Tales — 95% positive

The final game in the set is something completely different: a post-apocalyptic tactical RPG with turn-based combat and roguelite mechanics. Developed by Hardlane Studio, it has players leading a dysfunctional crew of survivors through a dangerous wasteland filled with mutants, robots, and hostile raiders.

The hook is a personality system that lets characters develop friendships, rivalries, and even romantic relationships based on player decisions. Add in customizable character classes, strategic combat, and Twitch integration, and there's plenty here to keep you busy. It carries a 95% positive rating on Steam.

Why this batch is worth grabbing

Free-game drops on Steam are nothing new, but a set where the lowest-rated game still sits at 95% positive is rare. These aren't throwaway titles — they're four polished indie games across four genres, and the price of admission is zero. Whether you're hunting for something to play this weekend or just adding to the ever-growing backlog, all four are worth claiming while they're free.

Wild -1.5 (+102): Kaprizov past 500 and rolling against the Sharks

By Matt

Minnesota is 11-3-3 and first in the Central, Kaprizov is rolling after reaching 500 career points on November 1, and the Sharks come to St. Paul at 7-9-1. Laying 1.5 at plus money is the value side.

Game slip

San Jose Sharks (7-9-1) at Minnesota Wild (11-3-3)

Friday, November 6, 2026 — 8:00 PM ET — Grand Casino Arena, Saint Paul, MN — NHL Network

Spread: Wild -1.5 (+102)

Total: 6.5

Moneyline: Wild -258 / Sharks +210

Goalies: Jesper Wallstedt (MIN) vs. Yaroslav Askarov (SJ)

Odds source: DraftKings numbers via BetLegend, updated Nov. 6, 12:32 AM PT

### Injury report

Minnesota: G Filip Gustavsson (hip) — injured reserve, out until November 19. D Brock Faber (skull fracture) — injured reserve, expected back November 10. D Jared Spurgeon (lower body) — injured reserve, expected back November 10. F Marcus Foligno (lower body) — injured reserve, expected back November 12. C Joel Eriksson Ek missed Thursday's practice but it was a maintenance day — he will play Friday.

San Jose: C Alexander Wennberg (upper body) — injured reserve, expected back November 7. LW Igor Chernyshov (upper body) — injured reserve, expected back November 14. LW Quentin Musty (upper body) — injured reserve, expected back December 1.

### Storylines

• Kirill Kaprizov reached 500 career points on November 1 — an empty-netter against Washington, his 15th goal of the season. He is the second player in Wild history to reach the mark, after Mikko Koivu, and did it in 413 games, tied for fourth-fastest among active players. His 14 goals in October led the NHL and earned him Second Star of the Month.

• Minnesota is 11-3-3, first in the Central Division, and just beat Philadelphia 4-2 on Tuesday behind Jesper Wallstedt's 25 saves. The top line — Kaprizov, Eriksson Ek, and Matt Boldy — is intact.

• San Jose is 7-9-1 and starting Yaroslav Askarov in net. The Sharks have been game, but they are a bottom-half team walking into one of the league's best home environments against a team chasing a milestone for its star.

• The Wild's blue line is banged up — Faber and Spurgeon both out — which is the one crack in the armor. Minnesota has managed it so far: 11 wins in 17 games with a plus goal differential that sits among the league's best.

The bet

Minnesota Wild -1.5 (+102, DraftKings)

The moneyline at -258 is unplayable. The puck line at plus money is the way in. Minnesota wins games by two or more at home as a matter of routine — this is a team that beat Washington 5-1 on the road last weekend and has won 11 of 17 — and San Jose's defense is not built to keep this close for sixty minutes. Kaprizov coming off the 500-point milestone in front of the home crowd is the kind of thing that turns a two-goal lead into a three-goal lead.

Why

The gap between these teams is real. Minnesota is first in the Central; San Jose is 7-9-1 and playing its second game in three nights on the road. The Wild's top line is healthy and producing, Wallstedt has been steady filling in for Gustavsson, and the Sharks' injury list hits their middle six. Laying 1.5 at plus money on the better team, at home, with their star rolling off a milestone — that is the value side of this board.

The honest caveat

The Wild's defense is genuinely thin right now. Faber (skull fracture) and Spurgeon are both out, and San Jose has enough skill to punish mistakes if Minnesota gets loose. A one-goal game is the nightmare scenario for a -1.5 ticket, and the Sharks have kept games close this season. If the Wild's depleted blue line has a bad night, this can end 3-2 and the ticket dies on the margin. But the price is plus money for a reason — and the reason is that Minnesota is supposed to win this game by two.

The pick

Minnesota Wild -1.5 (+102). The best team in the Central, at home, at plus money, with their star one point from history. That is the bet.

2-Leg Parlay (about +308): Doncic Over 35.5 + Wild -1.5

By Matt

Two plays, one story: the league's hottest scorer at plus money, and the Central's best team laying 1.5 at plus money. Together they pay about +308.

The legs

Leg 1 — Luka Doncic Over 35.5 points (+102)

Portland Trail Blazers at Los Angeles Lakers, 10:00 PM ET. Doncic is averaging a league-leading 38.5 points per game and has scored 37 or more in six straight games — 52, 41, 37, 45, 42, and 40. He dropped 41 on this same Portland team nine days ago. The Blazers are without Ja Morant, Shaedon Sharpe, and Vit Krejci, and the Lakers' offense runs entirely through Doncic in a revenge spot. Standard line 34.5 (-115 at Fanatics); the 35.5 alt pays plus money.

Leg 2 — Minnesota Wild -1.5 (+102)

San Jose Sharks at Minnesota Wild, 8:00 PM ET. The Wild are 11-3-3 and first in the Central. Kirill Kaprizov sits one point from 500 for his career — 244 goals, 255 assists in 412 games — and the home crowd will be pushing for it. San Jose is 7-9-1 with a banged-up middle six. The moneyline (-258) is unplayable; the puck line at plus money is the value side.

Why these two together

Both legs are plus money on their own, and neither asks for anything exotic. Doncic needs to do what he has done in six straight games. Minnesota needs to beat a 7-9-1 team by two goals at home. The parlay pays about +308 — a $10 ticket returns about $40.80 — because two plus-money legs multiply instead of adding.

The honest caveat

Parlays punish the one thing you cannot control: variance. Doncic can have a 34-point night and the Lakers can still win comfortably — that kills the ticket even though the read was right. Minnesota can win 3-2 and the puck line dies on the margin. Both legs are individually sound, but they have to land on the same night, and that is always the tax on a parlay. Bet it small, the way parlays are meant to be bet.

The pick

2-leg parlay, about +308: Luka Doncic Over 35.5 points (+102) + Minnesota Wild -1.5 (+102). The hottest scorer in the league and the best team in the Central, both at plus money. That is the bet.

If you or someone you know has a gambling problem, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

Collectibles · Nov 6, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

Pokémon TCG enters the National Toy Hall of Fame

By Matt

Thirty years after it debuted in Japan, the Pokémon Trading Card Game is a Hall of Famer — inducted Thursday alongside bingo, Care Bears, and the playground slide.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

The Pokémon Trading Card Game is officially a member of the National Toy Hall of Fame. The Strong National Museum of Play in Rochester, New York, announced the 2026 class on Thursday, November 5, on the Today show — and Pokémon TCG made the cut in its first year as a finalist.

It joins three other inductees: bingo, Care Bears, and the playground slide. The four winners came out of a field of 12 finalists that also included Bop It!, Mahjong, Monster High dolls, Mouse Trap, Polly Pocket, Sorry!, Tickle Me Elmo, and Yahtzee. All four are now enshrined and on display at The Strong.

Thirty years, 85 billion cards

The timing is the story. The Pokémon TCG debuted in Japan in 1996 — thirty years ago this year — after the Game Boy games turned monster-collecting into a phenomenon. The Hall of Fame, established in 1998, recognizes toys that "have inspired creative play and enjoyed popularity over a sustained period." Few inductees can match this one on the sustained part: the game has never really left the cultural conversation since.

The numbers back it up. As of March 2026, more than 85 billion Pokémon cards have been printed and distributed worldwide. The Strong's own write-up calls the game "renowned for its relative accessibility compared to other collectible card games" — a big reason it spread from schoolyards to a global hobby that now supports grading companies, billion-dollar resale markets, and release-day lines at every major retailer.

"Pokémon TCG's widespread appeal and global presence are undeniable," curator Mirek Stolee said in the announcement.

A very good week to be a Pokémon collector

The induction lands on the same week as the biggest release day of the anniversary year. Mega Evolution—Delta Reign hits shelves today, November 6, alongside the final wave of 30th Celebration collections. It is the kind of week that shows exactly why the Hall of Fame came calling: thirty years in, the game is still setting release-day records instead of coasting on nostalgia.

The other inductees tell their own version of the same story. Bingo descends from an Italian lottery played around 1530 and is now played virtually worldwide. Care Bears, like Pokémon, got a boost from an active fan community that mobilized around the nomination. And the slide — the simplest toy on the list — was honored for its role in child development and a century of playground use.

What to watch

The Strong says the induction will likely lead to new exhibits and events around the Pokémon TCG in the new year. For collectors, the near-term watch is simpler: this is the third piece of institutional recognition the hobby has picked up in a year when the cards themselves keep breaking records. The game is not just popular. It is, officially, historic.

New Balance 990v7 debuts today at $200

By Elijah

The seventh generation of New Balance's flagship 990 line arrives today — the Made in USA 990v7 in 'Kool Gray' for $200, with a campaign fronted by ROSALÍA, Cooper Flagg, and Josh Allen.

What happened

The New Balance Made in USA 990v7 debuts today, November 6. The seventh generation of the brand's flagship 990 franchise launches in the "Kool Gray" colorway for $200, available at 10 a.m. ET through New Balance and select retailers.

This is the drop the sneaker calendar has been building toward all month — DraftKings Network calls it the top sneaker drop of November 2026, and it is easy to see why. The 990 is the shoe that built New Balance's reputation with runners, dads, and fashion crowds all at once, and a new generation does not come around often.

The shoe

The 990v7 keeps the series' familiar suede-and-mesh construction — pig suede, tight-knit mesh, breathable mesh toe boxes — and streamlines the upper with updated proportions. Underneath, the cushioning setup pairs FuelCell with ENCAP, the two technologies New Balance has been building its performance line around.

The launch colorway, Kool Gray (style code U990GR7), is draped in the grey the brand is known for: Kool Gray, Calcium, and Light Arctic Gray, with a dark grey tumbled leather heel tab and a red "V7" lace holder on the leather tongue. It is, deliberately, the most New Balance-looking shoe New Balance could launch with — grey on grey on grey.

The campaign behind it is star-heavy: ROSALÍA, Cooper Flagg, Josh Allen, and IU front the launch. That is a deliberately broad net — pop, basketball, football, and K-pop — for a shoe that has always sold across tribes.

• Nike SB Dunk Low "Champagne" — $130 at select skate shops, with a wider SNKRS drop to follow. Style code IQ0431-300.

• Reebok DMX Trailzillo — two general-release colorways (blue/orange and black/white/neon green) at $120 via Reebok, an archive-inspired trail hybrid with the line's moving-air cushioning.

Why this one matters

The 990 line is New Balance's crown jewel, and the v6 had a long, beloved run. A new generation is a statement that the brand thinks it can improve on a classic without breaking what made it one — and the grey-on-grey launch colorway is a bet that the core audience wants evolution, not reinvention.

At $200, it is also priced like the flagship it is. If the Kool Gray sells through today, expect the 990v7 to anchor New Balance's holiday season the way the v6 did for years.

Gaming · Nov 6, 2026 · 7:54 AM ET

God of War Laufey delayed to May 20, 2027

By Matt

Santa Monica Studio pushed God of War Laufey from February 16 to May 20, 2027 — three more months of polish for Faye's first outing as the series lead, still a PS5 exclusive.

What happened

Santa Monica Studio is delaying God of War Laufey by three months. The game was set to launch February 16, 2027. It will now arrive May 20, 2027, still as a PlayStation 5 exclusive.

The studio announced the move Wednesday, November 4, in a short statement on X. No specific problem was named — the extra time, the studio said, goes to "putting in the finishing touches and pushing the game to its fullest potential." More updates are promised soon, with no date attached to them.

The timeline

This one moved fast from reveal to release date, which makes the delay sting a little less — and a little more.

• June 2026: Revealed at PlayStation's State of Play, closing the show. Faye — Laufey, Kratos' wife and Atreus' mother — wakes after her own funeral in the Everywhen, an afterlife where gods from different mythologies cross paths. Deborah Ann Woll plays Faye; Jack Quaid voices Phranque, a gelatinous cube traveling with her.

• July 2026: The February 16, 2027 date is confirmed at San Diego Comic-Con.

• September 29, 2026: Pre-orders open, with a Standard Edition and a Digital Deluxe Edition.

• November 4, 2026: The delay to May 20, 2027 — 93 days later.

That is a quick turnaround from announcement to launch by modern standards, and the February date lasted barely four months. If you pre-ordered, the studio's statement says nothing about pre-order terms, bonuses, or editions changing — treat those as unchanged until Sony says otherwise.

Why it matters

Laufey is the first God of War where you do not play Kratos. Faye leads, with a legendary sword, a Serpent's Bow, and combat built around speed and vertical movement — a deliberate break from the weightier Norse games. The reveal trailer ran more than 20 minutes of uncut gameplay, and it introduced two gods standing in Faye's way: Sekhmet and Begtse.

Santa Monica has done this before. God of War Ragnarök slipped from 2021 to November 2022 under similar "polish" framing, and it went on to be one of the best-reviewed games of its year. A 93-day delay this late in the cycle reads like a game that is content-complete and needs optimization and bug fixes — not a rescue job.

The new date also buys breathing room. February 2027 is crowded — Tomb Raider: Legacy of Atlantis lands February 12, Final Fantasy XIV: Evercold on February 23 — and Sony's own Until Dawn 2 arrives January 28. May is, for now, a much quieter month.

By Matt

Godzilla stomps into Apex Legends on November 10 — a Storm Point Wildcard takeover, a heat-ray Devotion, ten kaiju Legend skins, and a mythic Dorsal Dagger, running through December 1.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Godzilla is coming

Godzilla is stomping into Apex Legends. Respawn announced the Apex Legends | Godzilla Event today, November 5, and it runs November 10 through December 1 — three weeks of the King of the Monsters tearing up Storm Point. The tagline says it all: survive the monster, become a monster.

This is not a skins-only crossover. Godzilla is on the map, and you cannot kill it. You can only survive it.

Storm Point becomes Godzilla's stomping ground

The centerpiece is a Wildcard takeover of Storm Point. The map is mid-attack: damaged buildings, burnt trees, fallen signs, and destruction scattered across the island, with Godzilla looming above it all.

When Godzilla marks an area for destruction, a countdown starts before its Heat Ray strikes. Players brave enough to run into the blast zone before the beam hits can grab a Care Package holding a custom Devotion — a heat-ray weapon that tracks enemies on hit. Twitch Drops run November 10–17 with select creators.

The event is tied to Godzilla's return to theaters — Godzilla Minus Zero hits theaters this week, with a 3D re-release in IMAX and large format on November 6. The timing is no accident: the movie brings the monster back to the big screen, and Apex brings it to the Outlands the same week.

Respawn teased this one first. On November 2, the official Apex account posted a 17-second clip captioned "Something big is coming…" — a glowing Apex logo splitting into a row of shapes that turned out to be Godzilla's dorsal plates, with a roar at the end. Three days later, the full reveal landed.

Collectibles · Nov 5, 2026 · 11:40 AM ET

GameStop's $10,000 Pokémon pack gives you a 44.8% chance of pulling a card worth $6,000–$8,000

By Matt

GameStop's new Riptonium Power Pack costs $10,000 for one PSA-graded Pokémon card — and GameStop's own odds say nearly 75% of pulls come back worth less than the pack. The top chase is a $156,000 Lugia.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

GameStop has gone all in on the digital pack. The retailer announced Riptonium on October 10 — its biggest Power Pack tier yet — and the price is exactly what it sounds like: $10,000 for a single digital pack that reveals one PSA-graded Pokémon card.

The catch is right there on GameStop's own listing. According to the odds GameStop publishes for the pack, 44.8% of pulls land in the $6,000–$8,000 estimated value band. That is at least $2,000 less than the pack costs, before resale fees or the gap between an estimate and what a buyer can actually get for the card.

The odds, straight from GameStop

Here is the full value chart for Riptonium, as reported from GameStop's listing:

$6,000–$8,000: 44.8%

$8,000–$10,000: 29.9%

$10,000–$20,000: 22.9%

$20,000–$40,000: 1.5%

$40,000–$80,000: 0.5%

Above $80,000: 0.4%

Add it up and 74.7% of pulls come back worth less than the $10,000 pack price. Only 2.4% of pulls land above $20,000. The top pull currently listed is valued at $156,000 — a PSA 10 2005 #105 Lugia ex-Holo, per Resell Calendar's report on the pool.

GameStop warns on the listing that values can fluctuate and that the probabilities update every five minutes. The estimates are calculated from market data and feed GameStop's instant buyback offers, which pay 90% of the estimated value. So even a "break-even" pull at $10,000 is worth $9,000 the moment you take the buyback.

How Power Packs work

Riptonium is the top rung of a ladder GameStop has been building all year. Power Packs are digital mystery packs made in partnership with PSA: you buy a pack online, it opens digitally to reveal a real, physical, PSA-graded card, and that card goes into your PSA Vault — a secure, insured storage facility in Delaware — until you decide to ship it, sell it, or keep it stored. Every card was already graded and slabbed before you spent a dollar.

The platform launched to the public on April 15, 2026 with four categories — Pokémon, football, basketball, and baseball — and nine price tiers: Starter ($25), Silver ($50), Gold ($100), Sapphire ($250), Platinum ($500), Diamond ($1,000), Lunar ($2,500), Neutronium ($5,000), and Riptonium ($10,000). Higher tiers buy a deeper card pool and a higher ceiling. The category list has since grown to include One Piece and themed Pokémon packs like Rewind and On Fire.

GameStop added the $5,000 Neutronium tier after announcing its bid for eBay, and Riptonium now pushes the ceiling to $10,000. It is, by a wide margin, the most expensive single digital pack in the hobby.

Why people are buying it anyway

The math says most buyers lose money. The hobby says that was never the point.

Power Packs sell the rip — the moment of the reveal, live on a creator's stream, with a real slab on the line. GameStop publishes live pull data, so collectors can watch in real time what other people are hitting. And for the 0.4%, there is a $156,000 Lugia sitting at the top of the pool.

That is the same logic that has powered the hobby's biggest nights all year, from Logan Paul's $1.2 million vintage rip to the record $16.49 million Pikachu Illustrator sale. The difference is that GameStop has industrialized it: nine tiers, published odds, a vault, and a buyback button. The casino is open, the odds are printed on the door, and the line is still forming.
